T_{\pi\circ\Phi(0)}^{\ast}\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
. This subspace, is, in fact, the cotangent space to the configuration space at
(w_{1},\dots,w_{n})
. In other words, we have

T_{\pi\circ\Phi(0)}^{\ast}\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})\cong\jmath^{\ast}(T_{\pi\circ\Phi(0)}^{\ast}\mathfrak{S}_{g})\oplus T_{\pi\circ\Phi(0)}^{\ast}\mathscr{F}_{n}\left(\mathcal{D}\right).

(124)

It follows from Eq.(
90
) that a standard basis for
T_{\pi\circ\Phi(0)}^{\ast}\mathscr{F}_{n}\left(\mathcal{D}\right)
is given by the following meromorphic automorphic forms of weight four,

P_{3g-3+i}(w)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma}R(\sigma w,w_{i})\sigma^{\prime}(w)^{2},\qquad w\in\Omega^{\text{reg}},

(125)

which represent
\differential{w_i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
. According to the following pairing, which actually is an analog of pairing (
36
)

\left(Q,M\right)=\iint_{\mathcal{D}}Q(w)M(w)\differential[2]{w},

(126)

we can obtain the dual basis for
P_{1}(w),\dots P_{3g-3+n}(w)
— i.e. the basis
M_{1}(w),\dots M_{3g-3+n}(w)
in
\mathcal{H}^{-1,1}(\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\Omega},\Sigma)
which coincide with the tangent vectors
\partialderivative{w_1},\dots,\partialderivative{w_{n}}\in T_{\pi\circ\Phi(0)}\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}
. Similarly, the corresponding bases in the tangent and cotangent spaces to
\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}
at an arbitrary point can also be defined. This implies that
\text{Sch}\left(J^{-1};w\right)=\partial_{w}^{2}\varphi(w)-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{w}\varphi(w)\right)^{2}
can be decomposed as following
55
55

55

See the asymptotic behavior of
\varphi(w)
and its derivatives as
w\to w_{i}
in lemma
C.1
.

\text{Sch}\left(J^{-1};w\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}\mathscr{E}_{i}(w)-\pi\sum_{i=1}^{3g-3+n}c_{i}P_{i}(w),

(127)

where

\mathscr{E}_{i}(w)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\left(\frac{1}{(\sigma w-w_{i})^{2}}-\frac{1}{\sigma w(\sigma w-1)}\right)\sigma^{\prime}(w)^{2},\quad\text{for}\quad i=1,\dots,n,

(128)

are meromorphic automorphic forms of weight four for Schottky group with the second order poles at
\Sigma\cdot w_{i}
and

are accessory parameters. Moreover, for the variations of hyperbolic metric we have the same formulas introduced in subsection
3.1.2
and
3.2.1
. Furthermore, the formula (

) is also valid in this case. Finally, on
\mathcal{T}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
, each cuspidal
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\text{TZ},i}^{\text{cusp}}
and elliptic
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\text{TZ},i}^{\text{ell}}
metric remains invariant under the automorphism group of the covering
\pi:\mathcal{T}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})\to\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
. Accordingly, each metric descends to a Kähler metric on
\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
. All these also imply that in analogy with the commutative diagram (

\begin{CD}\mathbb{H}@>{f^{\mu}}>{}>\mathbb{H}\\
@V{J}V{}V@V{}V{J_{\mu}}V\\
\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\Omega}@>{F^{\mu}}>{}>\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\Omega}\hskip 1.13809pt^{\mu}\end{CD}

(129)

where

\partial_{\bar{w}}F^{\mu}=M(w)\hskip 2.84526pt\partial_{w}F^{\mu}\qquad\text{for}\quad w\in\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\Omega},

(130)

and
F^{\varepsilon\mu}
is complex-analytic in

and

is given by similar equation with Eq.(

) where
w\in\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\Omega}
.

Before closing this section let us also mention that the mapping
J
in diagram (
13
) has the following expansions near cusps and branch points of
\Gamma
(see Appendix
C
)

J(z)=\left\{\begin{split}&w_{i}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}J^{(i)}_{k}\left(\frac{z-z_{i}}{z-\bar{z}_{i}}\right)^{km_{i}}\hskip 95.3169pt(i=1,\dots,n_{e}),\hskip 5.69046ptz\to z_{i},\\
\\
&w_{i}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}J^{(i)}_{k}\exp(-\frac{2 \pi\hspace{1mm}\sqrt{-1}k}{|\delta_{i}|(z-z_{i})})\hskip 28.45274pt(i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1),\hskip 5.69046ptz\to z_{i},\end{split}\right.

(131)

where
w_{i}=J(z_{i})
for
i=1,\dots,n-1
.
56
56

56

Note that since
\Sigma
is normalized,
\infty\notin\Omega
.
. If
e^{\varphi(w)}|\differential{w}|^{2}
denotes the push-forward of the hyperbolic metric on
\mathbb{H}
by the map
J
, then the density of hyperbolic metric on
\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\Omega}
(i.e.
\rho(w)=e^{\varphi(w)}
) is once again given by Eq.(
77
), where
\varphi(w)
is smooth on
\Omega^{\text{reg}}
. The function
\varphi(w)
satisfies
57
57

57

This equation follows from the invariance of hyperbolic metric on
\Omega^{\text{reg}}
under the action of
\Sigma
— i.e.
e^{\varphi(w)}\differential{w}\differential{\bar{w}}=e^{\varphi(\sigma w)}\differential{\sigma(w)}\differential{\overline{\sigma(w)}}
for all
\sigma\in\Sigma
.

\varphi(\sigma w)=\varphi(w)-\log|\sigma^{\prime}(w)|^{2}\quad\text{for}\quad w\in\Omega^{\text{reg}},\,\forall\sigma\in\Sigma.

(132)

According to Lemma
C.1
, it also has the following asymptotic form

\varphi(w)=\left\{\begin{split}&-2(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}})\log|w-w_{i}|+\log\frac{4|J^{(i)}_{1}|^{-\frac{2}{m_{i}}}}{m_{i}^{2}}+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)\hskip 36.98866ptw\to w_{i},\\
&-2\log|w-w_{j}|-2\log\left|\log\left|\frac{w-w_{j}}{J^{(i)}_{1}}\right|\right|+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)\hskip 56.9055ptw\to w_{j},\\
&-2\log|w|-2\log\log\left|\frac{w}{J^{(n)}_{-1}}\right|+\order{|w|^{-1}},\hskip 79.6678ptw\to\infty,\end{split}\right.

(133)

for
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
and
j=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
.

Remark 3.10
.

It follows from the above asymptotics that (see statement 3 of lemma
C.1
for more details)

\left\{\begin{split}&\log\mathsf{h}_{i}=-2\log m_{i}+2\log 2-\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\left(\varphi(w)+\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)\log|w-w_{i}|^{2}\right),\hskip 5.69046pti=1,\dots,n_{e},\\
\\
&\log\mathsf{h}_{i}=\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\left(\log|w-w_{i}|^{2}-\frac{2e^{-\frac{\varphi(w)}{2}}}{|w-w_{i}|}\right),\hskip 56.9055pti=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1,\\
&\log\mathsf{h}_{n}=\lim_{w\to\infty}\left(\log|w|^{2}-\frac{2e^{-\frac{\varphi(w)}{2}}}{|w|}\right).\end{split}\right.

(134)

with
\mathsf{h}_{i}=\left|J^{(i)}_{1}\right|^{\frac{2}{m_{i}}}
for
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
, and
\mathsf{h}_{i}=\left|J^{(i)}_{1}\right|^{2}
for
i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n
.

Finally, consider the points
w_{1},\dots,L_{k}w_{i},\dots,w_{n}
, corresponding to the branch points and cusps in
z_{1},\dots,\beta_{k}z_{i},\dots,z_{n}
.
58
58

58

One can comprehend this by recognizing that
J\circ\beta_{k}=L_{k}\circ J
.
Near the point
\beta_{k}z_{i}
, the first coefficient in the expansion (
131
) of
J(z)
is given by
L^{\prime}_{k}(w_{i})J_{1}^{(i)}
. Accordingly, the positive functions
\mathsf{h}_{i}=\left|J^{(i)}_{1}\right|^{\frac{2}{m_{i}}}
for
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
and
\mathsf{h}_{i}=\left|J^{(i)}_{1}\right|^{2}
for
i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n
are respectively replaced with
\mathsf{h}_{i}\left|L^{\prime}_{k}(w_{i})\right|^{\frac{2}{m_{i}}}
and
\mathsf{h}_{i}\left|L^{\prime}_{k}(w_{i})\right|^{2}
, when sending
w_{i}
to
L_{k}w_{i}
. Moreover, let us define
\mathscr{L}_{i}
as the
i
-th relative cotangent line bundle on
\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
, situated along the fibers of the projection
p_{i}:\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})\to\mathfrak{S}_{g,n-1}(\boldsymbol{m})
.
59
59

59

This projection forgets the
w_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
.
Given this understanding,
we can establish the following assertion.

Lemma 3.5
.

Hermitian metrics in the holomorphic line bundles
\mathscr{L}_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
are determined by the quantities
\mathsf{h}_{i}^{m_{i}}
.

Proof.

To prove this lemma for the branch points, we use the transformation of
\mathsf{h}_{i}
’s under the action of the generators
L_{k}
of the Schottky group. As explained above, sending
w_{1},\dots,w_{i},\dots,w_{n_{e}}
to
w_{1},\dots,L_{k}w_{i},\dots,w_{n_{e}}
will result in
\mathsf{h}_{i}
to be replaced by
\mathsf{h}_{i}\left|L^{\prime}_{k}(w_{i})\right|^{\frac{2}{m_{i}}}
. Thus, we have:

\Delta\log\mathsf{h}_{i}=\frac{1}{m_{i}}\log\left|L^{\prime}_{k}(w_{i})\right|^{2}.

(135)

This means that
\mathsf{h}_{i}^{m_{i}}
is a Hermitian metric in the line bundle
\mathscr{L}_{i}
. For the case of cusp, a similar method of proof is applicable and we conclude that
\mathsf{h}_{i}
s will determine Hermitian metric in the line bundle
\mathscr{L}_{i}
.
∎

4
Classical Liouville Action

In this section, we study the classical Liouville action for hyperbolic Riemann orbisurfaces with the genus
g=0
and
g>1
, separately. Before proceeding, we should mention that all the proofs in sections
4
and
5
are previously provided for the case of punctured Riemann surfaces in
Zograf1988ONLE
and
park2015potentials
. When it comes to calculations involving punctures, we can only direct the reader to those articles. However, for the reader’s convenience and to facilitate a clearer understanding of distinctions in the presence of conical singularities, we find it appropriate to present all proofs side by side.

4.1
Riemann Orbisurfaces of Genus 0

Let
O
be a marked Riemann orbisurface with signature
(0;m_{1},\dots,m_{n_{e}},n_{p})
and let
\boldsymbol{m}
denotes the vector of orders
(m_{1},\dots,m_{n})
. The regularized action functional for the Liouville equation (
78
) in presence of conical singularities with conical angles
2\pi/m_{1},\dots,2\pi/m_{n_{e}}
at
w_{1},\dots,w_{n_{e}}
together with punctures at
w_{n_{e}+1},\dots,w_{n-2}=0,w_{n-1}=1,w_{n}=\infty
is defined as follows (see
Zograf1988ONLE
;
Takhtajan:1995fd
;
Takhtajan:2001uj
):

S_{\boldsymbol{m}}[\varphi]=\\
\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\left(\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{2\varphi})\differential[2]{w}+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)\oint_{C_{i}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{i}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{i}}\right)\right.\\
\left.-2\pi\sum_{i=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)^{2}\log\epsilon+2\pi n_{p}\log\epsilon+4\pi(n_{p}-2)\log|\log\epsilon|\right),

(136)

where
60
60

60

Note that
\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}O_{\epsilon}=O^{\text{reg}}=X_{O}^{\text{reg}}
.

O_{\epsilon}=\mathbb{C}\Big\backslash\bigcup_{i=1}^{n-1}\left\{w\,\Big|\,|w-w_{i}|<\epsilon\right\}\cup\left\{w\,\Big|\,|w|>\epsilon^{-1}\right\},

and the circles

C_{i}^{\epsilon}=\left\{w\,\Big|\,|w-w_{i}|=\epsilon\right\},

are oriented as a component of the boundary
\partial O_{\epsilon}
.

Remark 4.1
.

When
n_{p}=0
, the appropriate classical Liouville action is given by

S_{\boldsymbol{m}}[\varphi]=\\
\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\Bigg(\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{2\varphi})\differential[2]{w}+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)\oint_{C_{i}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{i}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{i}}\right)\\
+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{m_{n}}\right)\oint_{C_{n}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w}\right)-2\pi\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)^{2}\log\epsilon-2\pi\left(1+\frac{1}{m_{n}}\right)^{2}\log\epsilon\Bigg).

(137)

Remark 4.2
.

It is worth providing some explanation regarding the action (
136
) and why the terms associated with conical points appear different from those associated with the punctures. The contour integral in the first line of (
136
) is added to ensure a well-defined variational principle around the conical singularities.
61
61

61

Alternatively, the line integrals are necessary in order to ensure the proper asymptotic behavior (
133
). See the explanation following
(
Takhtajan:1995fd
, Eq. (8))
for more details.
The variation of this integral cancels the boundary term arising from the variation of the bulk term. To ensure a well-defined variational principle around the punctures, additional contour integrals must be considered, which are given by
(
Takhtajan:1995fd
, §2)

\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{j=n_{e}+1}^{n-1}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\text{c.c}\right)+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{j=n_{e}+1}^{n-1}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{(\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j})\log|w-w_{j}|}-\text{c.c.}\right)\\
-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\oint_{C_{n}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}}-\text{c.c}\right)-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\oint_{C_{n}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}\log|w|}-\text{c.c.}\right).

(138)

More concretely, the classical Liouville action (
136
) in the presence of these additional contour integrals takes the form
(
Takhtajan:1995fd
, Eq. (8))

S_{\boldsymbol{m}}[\varphi]=\\
\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\left(\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{2\varphi})\differential[2]{w}+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)\oint_{C_{i}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{i}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{i}}\right)\right.\\
+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{j=n_{e}+1}^{n-1}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{(\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j})\log|w-w_{j}|}-\frac{\differential{w}}{(w-w_{j})\log|w-w_{j}|}\right)-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\oint_{C_{n}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w}\right)\\
\left.-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\oint_{C_{n}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}\log|w|}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w\log|w|}\right)-2\pi\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)^{2}\log\epsilon\right),

(139)

where
m_{i}=\infty
for
i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n
. By substituting the asymptotic form (
133
) of the Liouville field
\varphi
near punctures in the contour integrals of (
139
), up to
\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)
terms, one gets

\begin{split}&\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{j=n_{e}+1}^{n-1}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\text{c.c}\right)+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{j=n_{e}+1}^{n-1}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{(\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j})\log|w-w_{j}|}-\text{c.c.}\right)\\
&-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\oint_{C_{n}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}}-\text{c.c}\right)-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\oint_{C_{n}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}\log|w|}-\text{c.c.}\right)=4\pi n_{p}\log\epsilon+4\pi(n_{p}-2)\log|\log\epsilon|.\end{split}

(140)

Since the contour integral (
138
) evaluated on-shell is merely a divergent term, we can add it to the counterterm
-2\pi n_{p}\log\epsilon
in (
139
) to get the Liouville action (
136
). The resulting regulating terms will have the opposite sign (i.e., plus sign instead of minus sign) and correctly cancel the divergence coming from the bulk term
\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\differential[2]{w}
.

Performing a similar calculation for the case of conical points, i.e., substituting the asymptotic form (
133
) of the Liouville field
\varphi
near the conical points in the contour integral of (
136
), one observes that the result is given by

\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)\oint_{C_{i}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{i}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{i}}\right)=4\pi\sum_{i=1}^{n_{e}}\big[(1-\tfrac{1}{m_{i}})^{2}\log\epsilon+\tfrac{1}{2}(1-\tfrac{1}{m_{i}})\log\mathsf{h}_{i}\big]+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right).

(141)

Notice that, apart from the divergent part, the above expression contains a finite part (i.e.,
2\pi\sum_{i}(1-\tfrac{1}{m_{i}})\log\mathsf{h}_{i}
) which behaves non-trivially under quasi-conformal transformations and therefore cannot be ignored (see lemma
5.1
for more details).
62
62

62

For both punctures and conical points, there are finite constant terms in the calculation of the contour integrals which can be safely ignored. These terms are not written in equations (
140
) and (
141
).
We have decided to keep the line integrals around conical points in their integral form since this form is more familiar in the literature on Liouville CFT and can also be used when the conical points are of a more general type (see, e.g.
Takhtajan:2001uj
). Therefore, one arrives at the classical Liouville action (
136
).

Theorem

(Takhtajan and Zograf)
.

For any fixed vector of orders
\boldsymbol{m}=(m_{1},\dots,m_{n})
such that
\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}(1-\tfrac{1}{m_{j}})+n_{p}>2
, the function
S_{\boldsymbol{m}}:\mathcal{M}_{0,n}\to\mathbb{R}
is differentiable and

c_{i}=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\partialderivative{S_{\boldsymbol{m}}}{w_i}\qquad\text{for all}\qquad i=1,\dots,n-3,

(142)

where
c_{i}
s are the accessory parameters defined by (
81
).
63
63

63

See Theorem 1 in
Zograf1988ONLE
for genus
g=0
punctured Riemann surfaces.

Proof.

Let

\tilde{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\epsilon}(w_{1},\dots,w_{n-3})=\tilde{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(B)\epsilon}(w_{1},\dots,w_{n-3})+\tilde{S}^{\text{(ct)}\epsilon}-2\pi\chi(X),

(143)

with
64

In view of the Gauss-Bonnet formula for Riemann orbisurfaces
Troyanov1991PrescribingCO

\\
\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\iint_{O}e^{\varphi}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}=2\pi\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)+n_{p}-2\right)=-2\pi\chi(O).

\tilde{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(B)\epsilon}(w_{1},\dots,w_{n-3})=\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\differential[2]{w}+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{i}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)\\
\tilde{S}^{(\text{ct})\epsilon}=-2\pi\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)^{2}\log\epsilon+2\pi(n-n_{e})\log\epsilon+4\pi(n-n_{e}-2)\log|\log\epsilon|.

(144)

For any
\epsilon>0
, the function
\tilde{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\epsilon}
is continuously differentiable on
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
. To prove this theorem, it suffices to show that
\mathcal{L}_{\mu_{i}}\tilde{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\epsilon}
converges uniformly to
-2\pi c_{i}
as
\epsilon\to 0
in a neighborhood of any point of the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
. More explicitly, one needs to show that

\mathcal{L}_{\mu_{i}}\tilde{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\epsilon}=\partialderivative{\tilde{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\epsilon}}{w_i}\overset{\text{\tiny\it def.}}{\equiv}\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\Bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}\tilde{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\epsilon}(w_{1}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}},\dots,w_{n-3}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})=-2\pi c_{i}\quad\text{for}\quad i=1,\dots,n-3,

(145)

pointwise on the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
.

We remind the reader that the basis
\{\mu_{i}\}
for
T_{\Phi(0)}\mathcal{T}(\Gamma)
has been defined in Eq.(
92
).
Firstly, we can write

\partialderivative{\tilde{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(B)\epsilon}}{w_i}=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\Bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}I_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon),

(146)

where

\begin{split}&I_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)=\left(I_{\epsilon}[\varphi]\circ\Psi\circ\Phi\right)(\varepsilon\mu_{i}),\\
&\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(w)=\varphi\Big(w;(\Psi\circ\Phi)(\varepsilon\mu_{i})\Big)=\varphi(w;\underbrace{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(w_{1})}_{w_{1}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}},\dots,\underbrace{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(w_{n-3})}_{w_{n-3}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}),\end{split}

and

I_{\epsilon}[\varphi]=\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{i}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right).

Accordingly, one gets

\begin{split}I_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)=&\iint_{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(O_{\epsilon})}|\partial_{w}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(w)|^{2}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(C_{j}^{\epsilon})}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(w)\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\overline{w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\right),\end{split}

(147)

with

\left\{\begin{split}&F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(O_{\epsilon})=\mathbb{C}\Big\backslash\bigcup_{k=1}^{n-1}\left\{w\,\Big|\,|w-w_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|<\epsilon\right\}\cup\left\{w\,\Big|\,|w|>\epsilon^{-1}\right\},\\
&F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(C_{j}^{\epsilon})=\left\{w\,\Big|\,|w-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|=\epsilon\right\}.\end{split}\right.

(148)

The calculation of (
146
) can be done by almost verbatim repeating the corresponding computations in the proof of Theorem 1 in
Zograf1988ONLE
. Accordingly, let us now use the
change of variable formula
for differential forms,
\int_{F(X)}\omega=\int_{X}F^{\ast}(\omega)
, and the commutative diagram (
84
) to write

\begin{split}I_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)&=\iint_{O_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}(F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\ast}\Big(|\partial_{w}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\Big)\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}(F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\ast}\left(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\overline{w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\right)\right)\\
&=\iint_{O_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}|\partial_{w}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}\differential{F^{\varepsilon\mu_i}(w)}\wedge\differential{\overline{F^{\varepsilon\mu_i}(w)}}\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})\left(\frac{\differential{\overline{F^{\varepsilon\mu_i}(w)}}}{\overline{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(w)}-\overline{w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}-\frac{\differential{F^{\varepsilon\mu_i}(w)}}{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(w)-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\right)\\
&=\iint_{O_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}|\partial_{w}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}\,|\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}(1-|\varepsilon M_{i}|^{2})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})\left(\frac{\overline{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}(\bar{\varepsilon}\overline{M_{i}}\differential{w}+\differential{\bar{w}})}{\overline{w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}-\overline{w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}-\frac{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(\differential{w}+\varepsilon M_{i}\differential{\bar{w}})}{w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\right),\end{split}

where

\left\{\begin{split}&O_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)=\mathbb{C}\Big\backslash\bigcup_{k=1}^{n-1}\left\{w\,\Big|\,|w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}-w_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|<\epsilon\right\}\cup\left\{w\,\Big|\,|w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|>\epsilon^{-1}\right\},\\
&C_{j}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)=\left\{w\,\Big|\,|w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|=\epsilon\right\}.\end{split}\right.

(149)

In order to compute
\partial I_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)/\partial\varepsilon|{\varepsilon=0}
, it is necessary to differentiate both the integrand and the integration domains
O_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)
and
C_{j}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)
:

\begin{split}\partialderivative{\varepsilon}&\Bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}I_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)=\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\Bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}|\partial_{w}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}\,|\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}(1-|\varepsilon M_{i}|^{2})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&+\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\Bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}\iint_{O_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\Bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})\left(\frac{\overline{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}(\bar{\varepsilon}\overline{M_{i}}\differential{w}+\differential{\bar{w}})}{\overline{w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}-\overline{w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}-\frac{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(\differential{w}+\varepsilon M_{i}\differential{\bar{w}})}{w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\right)\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\Bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right).\end{split}

(150)

The second and fourth terms in Eq.(
150
) can be computed using the formula for differentiating a given
k
-form
\omega
over a smooth family of variable domains
O(\varepsilon)

\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\Bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}\underbrace{\int\dotsm\int_{O_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}}_{k}\omega=\underbrace{\int\dotsm\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\partial O_{\epsilon}}}}_{k-1}i_{V}(\omega),

(151)

where

denotes the interior product of the
k
-form
\omega
with vector field
V
which is the vector field along
\partial O_{\epsilon}
corresponding to the family of curves
\partial O_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)
. As a result, we get

\begin{split}\partialderivative{I_{\epsilon}}{w_i}&=\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}\left[(\partial_{w_{i}}\partial_{w}\varphi+\partial_{w}^{2}\varphi\dot{F}^{i})\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi+(\partial_{w_{i}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi+\partial_{w}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\dot{F}^{i})\partial_{w}\varphi+|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\right]\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&\hskip-17.07182pt-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\int_{\partial O^{\epsilon}_{k}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\Big(\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(w_{k})\Big)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&\hskip-17.07182pt+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\Bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})\hskip-2.84526pt\left(\frac{\overline{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}(\bar{\varepsilon}\overline{M_{i}}\differential{w}+\differential{\bar{w}})}{\overline{w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}-\overline{w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}-\frac{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(\differential{w}+\varepsilon M_{i}\differential{\bar{w}})}{w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\right),\end{split}

(152)

where the last term in Eq.(
150
) has vanished due to the fact that
\partial C_{j}^{\epsilon}=\emptyset
. By noting that

\begin{split}&\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{d\bar{w}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{dw}{w-w_{j}}\right)=\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt-2\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{dw}{w-w_{j}}\right)-\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\log|w-w_{j}|^{2}dw-\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\log|w-w_{j}|^{2}d\bar{w},\end{split}

we have

\begin{split}&\hskip-22.76228pt\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})\left(\frac{\overline{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}(\bar{\varepsilon}\overline{M_{i}}\differential{w}+\differential{\bar{w}})}{\overline{w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}-\overline{w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}-\frac{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(\differential{w}+\varepsilon M_{i}\differential{\bar{w}})}{w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\right)\\
&=\underbrace{-2\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})\left(\frac{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(dw+\varepsilon M_{i}d\bar{w})}{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(w)-F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(w_{j})}\right)}_{B_{1}}\\

&\underbrace{-\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\left(\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\right)\log|F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(w)-F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(w_{j})|^{2}\hskip 2.84526pt\overline{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\left(\bar{\varepsilon}\overline{M_{i}}dw+d\bar{w}\right)}_{B_{3}}.\end{split}

After simple calculations and using the Lemma
3.4
, one can see that

\begin{split}&\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\Bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}B_{1}=2\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w)\left(\frac{dw}{w-w_{j}}\right)-2\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{M_{i}\hskip 2.84526ptd\bar{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)\\
&\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\Bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}B_{2}=\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial^{2}_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\log|w-w_{j}|^{2}dw-\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})dw\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt-\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\hskip 2.84526ptM_{i}\log|w-w_{j}|^{2}d\bar{w}+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right),\\
&\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\Bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}B_{3}=\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\hskip 2.84526ptM_{i}\log|w-w_{j}|^{2}d\bar{w}+\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\log|w-w_{j}|^{2}d\bar{w}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt-\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})d\bar{w}+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right),\end{split}

as
\epsilon\to 0
. This implies that

\begin{split}&\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\Bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}\left(B_{1}+B_{2}+B_{3}\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt=-\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\left(\frac{d\bar{w}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{dw}{w-w_{j}}\right)-\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})d\varphi-2\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{M_{i}\hskip 2.84526ptd\bar{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\overset{\eqref{MAsymptotic}}{=}-\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\left(\frac{d\bar{w}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{dw}{w-w_{j}}\right)+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right).\end{split}

Therefore, according to the above result, for the third term in (

) we get

\begin{split}&\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\Bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})\left(\frac{\overline{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}(\bar{\varepsilon}\overline{M_{i}}\differential{w}+\differential{\bar{w}})}{\overline{w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}-\overline{w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}-\frac{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(\differential{w}+\varepsilon M_{i}\differential{\bar{w}})}{w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt=-\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\left(\frac{d\bar{w}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{dw}{w-w_{j}}\right)+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right).\end{split}

(153)

Accordingly, the Eq.(

) can be further simplified to get

\begin{split}\partialderivative{I_{\epsilon}}{w_i}&=\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}\left[(\partial_{w_{i}}\partial_{w}\varphi+\partial_{w}^{2}\varphi\dot{F}^{i})\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi+(\partial_{w_{i}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi+\partial_{w}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\dot{F}^{i})\partial_{w}\varphi+|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\right]\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\int_{\partial O^{\epsilon}_{k}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\Big(\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(w_{k})\Big)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right).\end{split}

(154)

Next, using the Lemma
3.4
, we have

\left\{\begin{split}&\partial_{w_{i}}\partial_{w}\varphi+\partial_{w}^{2}\varphi\,\dot{F}^{i}=-\partial_{w}\varphi\,\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}-\partial_{w}^{2}\dot{F}^{i},\\
\\
&\partial_{w_{i}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi+\partial_{w}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\dot{F}^{i}=-\partial_{w}\varphi\,\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}-\partial_{\bar{w}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i},\end{split}\right.

(155)

which makes it possible to rewrite (

\begin{split}\partialderivative{I_{\epsilon}}{w_i}&=\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}\left[(-\partial_{w}\varphi\,\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}-\partial_{w}^{2}\dot{F}^{i})\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi+(-\partial_{w}\varphi\,\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}-\partial_{\bar{w}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i})\partial_{w}\varphi+|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\right]\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\int_{\partial O^{\epsilon}_{k}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\Big(\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(w_{k})\Big)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)\\
&=\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}\left[\Big(2\partial_{w}^{2}\varphi-(\partial_{w}\varphi)^{2}\Big)\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}-2\partialderivative{w}(\partial_w \varphi\, \partial_{\bar{w}} \dot{F}^i)+\partialderivative{\bar{w}}(\partial_{w} \varphi\, \partial_{w}\dot{F}^i)-\partialderivative{w}(\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\, \partial_{w} \dot{F}^i)\right]\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\int_{\partial O^{\epsilon}_{k}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\Big(\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(w_{k})\Big)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)\\
&=\underbrace{\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}\Big(2\partial_{w}^{2}\varphi-(\partial_{w}\varphi)^{2}\Big)\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}}_{I_{1}}\underbrace{-2\int_{\partial O_{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\,\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}}_{I_{2}}\underbrace{-\int_{\partial O_{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\,\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w}}_{I_{3}}\\
&\underbrace{-\int_{\partial O_{\epsilon}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\,\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}}_{I_{4}}\underbrace{-\int_{\partial O_{\epsilon}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}}_{I_{5}}+\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{i}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right).\end{split}

(156)

Let us compute each of the integrals
I_{1},\dots,I_{5}
separately using Lemma
C.1
and Corollary
3.1
as well as equations (
79
) and (
80
). We begin with the integral

:

\begin{split}I_{1}&=\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}\Big(2\partial_{w}^{2}\varphi-(\partial_{w}\varphi)^{2}\Big)\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}=-2\int_{\partial O_{\epsilon}}T_{\varphi}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w}\\
&=-2\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{j}}\left(\frac{h_{j}}{2(w-w_{j})^{2}}+\frac{c_{j}}{w-w_{j}}+\dotsm\right)\left(\delta_{ij}+(w-w_{j})\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})+\dotsm\right)\differential{w}\\
&-2\sum_{j=n_{e}+1}^{n-1}\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{j}}\left(\frac{1}{2(w-w_{j})^{2}}+\frac{c_{j}}{w-w_{j}}+\dotsm\right)\left(\delta_{ij}+(w-w_{j})\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})+\dotsm\right)\differential{w}\\
&-2\oint_{C_{n}^{\epsilon}}\left(\frac{1}{2w^{2}}+\frac{c_{n}}{w^{3}}+\dotsm\right)\left(w\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(\infty)+\dotsm\right)\differential{w}\\
&=4\pi\sqrt{-1}c_{i}+2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}h_{k}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{k})-2\pi\sqrt{-1}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(\infty)+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)\quad\text{as}\quad\epsilon\to 0.\end{split}

In the last line we have used the notation
h_{k}=1-1/m_{k}^{2}
for
k=1,\dots,n_{e}
and
h_{k}=1
for
k=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
. In addition, we have

\begin{split}I_{2}&=-2\int_{\partial O_{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\,\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}=-2\int_{\partial O_{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi(w)\,M_{i}(w)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&\hskip-8.5359pt\overset{\eqref{MAsymptotic}}{=}-2\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{j}}\left(-\frac{1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}}{w-w_{j}}+\frac{c_{j}}{1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}}+\dotsm\right)\left(\frac{\bar{q}^{(j)}_{1}}{4\bar{J}^{(j)}_{1}}\cdot|w-w_{j}|^{1-\frac{2}{m_{j}}}+\dotsm\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-2\sum_{j=n_{e}+1}^{n-1}\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{j}}\left(-\frac{1}{w-w_{j}}\left(1+\frac{1}{\log\left|\frac{w-w_{j}}{J_{1}^{(j)}}\right|}\right)+c_{j}+\dotsm\right)\times\\
&\hskip 76.82234pt\times\left(-\frac{|\delta_{j}|^{2}\bar{q}_{1}^{(j)}}{4\pi^{2}\bar{J}_{1}^{(j)}}\cdot|w-w_{j}|\log^{2}|w-w_{j}|+\dotsm\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-2\oint_{C_{n}^{\epsilon}}\left(-\frac{1}{w}\left(1+\frac{1}{\log\left|\frac{w}{J_{-1}^{(n)}}\right|}\right)-\frac{c_{n}}{w^{2}}+\dotsm\right)\left(-\frac{|\delta_{n}|^{2}\bar{q}_{1}^{(n)}\bar{J}_{-1}^{(n)}}{4\pi^{2}}\cdot\frac{\log^{2}|w|}{|w|}+\dotsm\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&=\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right),\end{split}

as
\epsilon\to 0
. Let us now calculate
I_{3}+I_{4}
:

\begin{split}I_{3}+I_{4}&=-\int_{\partial O_{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\,\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w}-\int_{\partial O_{\epsilon}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\,\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}=-\int_{\partial O_{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\varphi}=\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right),\end{split}

as
\epsilon\to 0
. Finally, we can turn to calculating
I_{5}
:

\begin{split}I_{5}&=-\int_{\partial O_{\epsilon}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&=-\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{k}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\left(\delta_{ik}+(w-w_{k})\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{k})+\dotsm\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt-\oint_{C_{n}^{\epsilon}}\left(\frac{1}{|w|^{2}}+\dotsm\right)\left(w\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(\infty)+\dotsm\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&=-\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{i}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\differential{\bar{w}}-\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{k}}\left(\frac{\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{k}}\right)^{2}}{|w-w_{k}|^{2}}+\dotsm\right)\left((w-w_{k})\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{k})+\dotsm\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt-\oint_{C_{n}^{\epsilon}}\left(\frac{1}{|w|^{2}}+\dotsm\right)\left(w\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(\infty)+\dotsm\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&=-\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{i}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\differential{\bar{w}}-2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{k}}\right)^{2}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{k})+2\pi\sqrt{-1}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(\infty)+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right),\end{split}

as
\epsilon\to 0
with
m_{k}=\infty
for
k=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
. As a result, we have

I_{1}+\dotsm+I_{5}=4\pi\sqrt{-1}c_{i}-\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{i}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\differential{\bar{w}}+2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(-\frac{2}{m_{j}^{2}}+\frac{2}{m_{j}}\right)\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right),

(157)

as
\epsilon\to 0
. Moreover, by changing the conformal structure, the variation of counterterm action
\tilde{S}^{\epsilon}_{\text{ct}}
gives

\begin{split}&\partialderivative{\tilde{S}_{\text{ct}}^{\epsilon}}{w_i}=-2\pi\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)^{2}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)\hskip 14.22636pt\text{as}\hskip 8.5359pt\epsilon\rightarrow 0.\end{split}

(158)

Notice that we have only taken into account the contributions coming from the regulating terms for conical points and not the punctures. The reason for this can be traced back to the equation (
138
). By doing similar analysis as in equation (
153
) for the contour integrals in (
138
), it is easy to see that the quasi-conformal transformations of these contour integrals are canceled by those of the counterterm
-2\pi n_{p}\log\epsilon
. In other words, it is sufficient to only consider the quasi-conformal transformations of the bulk term for the case of punctures, which is in agreement with the analysis in
Zograf1988ONLE
.

Now, using equations (
156
), (
157
), (
143
), (
158
), and putting everything together, we arrive at our desired result

\begin{split}\partialderivative{\tilde{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\epsilon}}{w_i}&=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\left(4\pi\sqrt{-1}c_{i}-\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{i}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\differential{\bar{w}}+2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\left(\frac{2}{m_{j}}\right)\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})\right.\\
&\left.+\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{i}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\differential{\bar{w}}-2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\left(\frac{2}{m_{j}}\right)\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)\right)\\
&=-2\pi c_{i}+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)\quad\text{as}\quad\epsilon\to 0.\end{split}

In order to complete the proof, it remains to show that the remainder in the above formula can be estimated uniformly in a neighborhood of an arbitrary point
(w_{1},\dots,w_{n-3})\in\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
. Let
\Gamma
be a Fuchsian group uniformizing the orbisurface
O
. Then, the Hauptmodule
J_{\mu}
, where
\mu=t_{1}\mu_{1}+\dotsm+t_{n-3}\hskip 2.84526pt\mu_{n-3}\in\mathcal{H}^{-1,1}(\mathbb{H},\Gamma)
, is continuously differentiable with respect to the Bers’ coordinates
t_{i}
and its coefficients in an expansion similar to Eq.(
60
) have the same property. From this, we can conclude with the help of Eq.(
77
) and Lemma
3.3
that the remainders in assertions (2) and (4) of Lemma
C.1
can be estimated uniformly. Using the commutative diagram (
84
), we can conclude that an analogous assertion is valid also for the remainders in Corollary
3.1
for
\dot{F}^{i}
, and this completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

Theorem
142
means that
\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}(c_{i}\differential{w_i}+\bar{c}_{i}\differential{\bar{w}_i})
is an exact 1-form on
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
with anti-derivative
-S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/2\pi
.

In addition to Theorem
142
, we can also generalize
(
Zograf1988ONLE
, Theorem 2)
to include branch points: The Weil-Petersson metric defined on
\mathcal{T}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
in Sec.
3.1.3
can be projected onto
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
, since it is invariant under
\operatorname{Aut}(\Psi)
. We will continue to call this metric obtained on
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
as the Weil-Petersson metric and denote it with the same notation
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{{}_{\text{WP}}}
. Then, we have the following theorem:

Theorem

(Takhtajan and Zograf)
.

For any fixed vector of orders
\boldsymbol{m}=(m_{1},\dots,m_{n})
such that
\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}(1-\tfrac{1}{m_{j}})+n_{p}>2
, the function
-S_{\boldsymbol{m}}
is a real-analytic Kähler potential for the metric
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{{}_{WP}}
on
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
:
66
66

66

See Theorem 2 in
Zograf1988ONLE
for the genus
g=0
punctured Riemann surfaces.

\bar{\partial}\partial S_{\boldsymbol{m}}=-2\sqrt{-1}\hskip 2.84526pt\omega_{\text{WP}}.

(159)

Proof.

In order to prove this theorem, we have to prove that the accessory parameters
c_{1},\dots,c_{n-3}
are continuously differentiable on
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
, and

\partialderivative{c_i}{\bar{w}_j}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\left\langle\partialderivative{w_i},\partialderivative{w_j}\right\rangle_{{}_{\text{WP}}}\quad\text{for}\quad i,j=1,\dots,n-3.

(160)

The proof of continuous differentiability of the functions
c_{i}
on
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
follows readily from the definition of accessory parameters (
81
) and continuous differentiability of the Hauptmodule
J
with respect to the Ber’s coordinates. We now turn to proving Eq.(
160
) in the same way as for Theorem 2 in
Zograf1988ONLE
where no branch point exists. Let
(w_{1},\dots,w_{n-3})
be an arbitrary point in
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
and let
\Gamma
be a Fuchsian group uniformizing the orbisurface
O
. It follows from the commutative diagram (
84
) that

\text{Sch}\left(J_{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}^{-1}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}};w\right)=\text{Sch}\left(f^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}\circ J^{-1};z\right),

where
\mu_{j}
is an element of the basis in
\mathcal{H}^{-1,1}(\mathbb{H},\Gamma)
given by Eq.(
92
), and
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{C}
is sufficiently small. By using the following well-known property of Schwarzian derivative,

\text{Sch}\left(A\circ B;w\right)=\text{Sch}\left(A;w\right)\circ B\hskip 2.84526ptB^{\prime 2}+\text{Sch}\left(B;w\right)

we have

\text{Sch}\left(J_{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}^{-1};w\right)\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}\left(\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}\right)^{2}+\text{Sch}\left(F^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}};w\right)=\text{Sch}\left(f^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}};z\right)\circ J^{-1}(J^{-1})^{\prime 2}+\text{Sch}\left(J^{-1};z\right).

We can now differentiate both sides of the above equality with respect to
\bar{\varepsilon}
at the point
\varepsilon=0
, using Eq.(
79
) and the fact that
F^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}
, and as a result
w_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}
, are holomorphic functions of
\varepsilon
at
\varepsilon=0
. The left-hand side gives

\begin{split}&\left.\partialderivative{\bar{\varepsilon}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\left(\text{Sch}\left(J_{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}^{-1};w\right)\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}\left(\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}\right)^{2}+\text{Sch}\left(F^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}};w\right)\right)\\
&\hskip 22.76228pt=\left.\partialderivative{T_{\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_j}}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+\partial_{w}T_{\varphi}\left.\partialderivative{F^{\varepsilon\mu_j}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}+T_{\varphi}\left.\partialderivative{\bar{\varepsilon}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\left(\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}\right)^{2}+\left.\partialderivative{\bar{\varepsilon}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\text{Sch}\left(F^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}};w\right)\\
&\hskip 22.76228pt=\left.\partialderivative{\bar{\varepsilon}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\left(\frac{h_{i}}{2(w-w_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}})^{2}}+\frac{c_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}}{w-w_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\left(\left.\partialderivative{c_i^{\varepsilon\mu_j}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right)\frac{1}{w-w_{i}},\end{split}

(161)

where
c_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}=c_{i}(w_{1}^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}},\dots,w_{n-3}^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}})
. To compute the right-hand side, we will use the definition of Schwarzian derivative (
16
), the fact that
\partial/\partial{\bar{\varepsilon}}
of the functions
\partial_{z}f^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}
and
\partial_{z}^{2}f^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}
vanishes at
\varepsilon=0
, and the well-known Ahlfors formula
67
67

67

The Ahlfors formula can be derived by comparing the expression for
\dot{f}^{\mu_{j}}_{-}(z)
, given by Eq.(
88
), and the equality
\varLambda\varLambda^{\ast}=\operatorname{id}
discussed in Section
3.1.1
.

Ahlfors_quasiconformal_06
;
Ahlfors1961SomeRO

\left.\partialderivative{\bar{\varepsilon}}\partial_{z}^{3}f^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=-\frac{1}{2}q_{j},

where
\mu_{j}
and
q_{j}
are connected by the relation (
92
). Then, we have

\begin{split}&\left.\partialderivative{\bar{\varepsilon}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\bigg(\text{Sch}\left(f^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}};z\right)\circ J^{-1}(J^{-1})^{\prime 2}+\text{Sch}\left(J^{-1};z\right)\bigg)\\
&\hskip 34.14322pt=\left.\partialderivative{\bar{\varepsilon}}\left[\partial_{z}^{3}f^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}\circ J^{-1}(J^{-1})^{\prime 2}\right]\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=-\frac{1}{2}q_{j}\circ J^{-1}(J^{-1})^{\prime 2}=-\frac{1}{2}Q_{j}.\end{split}

(162)

Equating the left-hand side, (
161
), and the right-hand side, (
162
), and using Eq.(
82
), we get

\begin{split}&\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\left(\left.\partialderivative{c_i^{\varepsilon\mu_j}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right)\frac{1}{w-w_{i}}\\
&\hskip 8.5359pt=\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}\left(\left.\partialderivative{c_i^{\varepsilon\mu_j}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right)\frac{1}{w-w_{i}}+\left(\left.\partialderivative{\bar{\varepsilon}}c_{n-2}^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right)\frac{1}{w}+\left(\left.\partialderivative{\bar{\varepsilon}}c_{n-1}^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right)\frac{1}{w-1}\\
&\hskip 8.5359pt=\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}\left(\left.\partialderivative{c_i^{\varepsilon\mu_j}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right)\frac{1}{w-w_{i}}+\left(\left.\partialderivative{\bar{\varepsilon}}\left(-1+\frac{n}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}c_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}(w_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}-1)\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right)\frac{1}{w}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\left(\left.\partialderivative{\bar{\varepsilon}}\left(1-\frac{n}{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}c_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}w_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_{j}}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right)\frac{1}{w-1}\\
&\hskip 8.5359pt=\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}\left(\left.\partialderivative{c_i^{\varepsilon\mu_j}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right)\left(\frac{1}{w-w_{i}}+\frac{w_{i}-1}{w}-\frac{w_{i}}{w-1}\right)\overset{\eqref{Ridef}}{=}-\pi\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}\left(\left.\partialderivative{c_i^{\varepsilon\mu_j}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right)R_{i}=-\frac{1}{2}Q_{j},\end{split}

or

\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}\left(\left.\partialderivative{c_i^{\varepsilon\mu_j}}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\right)R_{i}=\frac{1}{2\pi}Q_{j}.

It now follows from the biorthogonality of the bases
R_{i}
and
Q_{j}
as well as Lemma
3.3
that

\partialderivative{c_i}{\bar{w}_j}=\frac{1}{2\pi}\langle Q_{i},Q_{j}\rangle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\left\langle\partialderivative{w_i},\partialderivative{w_j}\right\rangle_{{}_{\text{WP}}},

which is what we wanted. Combining Eq.(
160
) and Theorem
142
, we can conclude that the real-valued function
-S_{\boldsymbol{m}}
is a potential for the Weil-Petersson metric on
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}

-\partialderivative{S_{\boldsymbol{m}}}{w_i}{\bar{w}_j}=\left\langle\partialderivative{w_i},\partialderivative{w_j}\right\rangle_{{}_{\text{WP}}},

while the function
-S_{\boldsymbol{m}}\circ\Psi
is a potential for the Weil-Petersson metric on the Teichmüller space
\mathcal{T}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
. In addition, the real analyticity of the Weil-Petersson metric (see
Ahlfors1961SomeRO
) implies the real analyticity of the accessory parameters
c_{1},\dots,c_{n-3}
and of their generating function
-S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/2\pi
on the space
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
.
∎

Remark 4.4
.

Equations (
142
) and (
159
) also give us a proof that the Weil-Petersson metric is a Kähler metric.

Finally, let
\mathfrak{M}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{m})=\mathcal{M}_{0,n}/\operatorname{Symm}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{s}}\right)
be the moduli space of orbifold Riemann surfaces with signature
(0;m_{1},\dots,m_{n_{e}};n_{p})
. One can generalize the results of
(
Zograf_1990
, Sec.1)
to prove the following Lemma:

Lemma 4.1
.

A Hermitian metric in a holomorphic
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
\lambda_{0,\boldsymbol{m}}
over
\mathfrak{M}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
is determined by
\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/\pi]
, so that

\mathsf{c}_{1}\left(\lambda_{0,\boldsymbol{m}},\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/\pi]\right)=\frac{1}{\pi^{2}}\omega_{WP}.

(163)

Proof.

We first need to show that
\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/\pi]
is a hermitian metric in the holomorphic
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
\lambda_{0,\boldsymbol{m}}
defined in the Section
3.2
. To do so, we use the same representation of
\operatorname{Symm}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{s}}\right)
introduced there. For our purposes, it suffices to prove this lemma for the case where the signature of orbifold Riemann surface
O
is given by
(0;\underbrace{m,\dots,m}_{s},\underbrace{m^{\prime},\dots,m^{\prime}}_{s^{\prime}})
with
s\equiv s_{m}
and
s^{\prime}\equiv s_{m^{\prime}}>3
. Namely, we have only two kinds of points,
s
branch points of order
m
and
s^{\prime}
branch points of order
m^{\prime}
. Furthermore, we will fix the last three points with order
m^{\prime}>m
to be at
0,1,\infty
. Then, the generators and 1-cocycles of
\operatorname{Symm}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{s}}\right)=\operatorname{Symm}\left(s\right)\times\operatorname{Symm}\left(s^{\prime}\right)
would be the same as the ones we introduced in Section
3.2
. Moreover, in this case we have

S_{\boldsymbol{m}}[\varphi]=\\
\hskip 14.22636pt\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\left(\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{\varphi})\differential[2]{w}+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{s+s^{\prime}-1}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{k}}\right)\oint_{C_{k}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{k}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{k}}\right)\right.\\
\left.\hskip-28.45274pt+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{m^{\prime}}\right)\oint_{C_{s+s^{\prime}}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w}\right)\right),

(164)

where

\left\{\begin{split}&O_{\epsilon}=\mathbb{C}\Big\backslash\bigcup_{k=1}^{s+s^{\prime}-1}\left\{w\,\Big|\,|w-w_{k}|<\epsilon\right\}\cup\left\{w\,\Big|\,|w|>\epsilon^{-1}\right\},\\
&C_{k}^{\epsilon}=\left\{w\,\Big|\,|w-w_{k}|=\epsilon\right\},\hskip 14.22636ptk=1,2,\dots,s+s^{\prime}-1\\
&C_{s+s^{\prime}}^{\epsilon}=\left\{w\,\Big|\,|w|=1/\epsilon\right\},\end{split}\right.

(165)

and the circles are oriented as a component of
\partial O_{\epsilon}
. Notice that we did not consider the counterterms in (
164
). Actually,
\operatorname{Symm}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{s}}\right)
does not change the conformal family, and therefore the counterterms will not contribute to the variation under the action of this group, and therefore writing them would be redundant.
To study the variation of
S_{\boldsymbol{m}}[\varphi]
under the action of
\operatorname{Symm}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{s}}\right)
, it suffices to study its variation under the generators
\{(\sigma_{j,j+1},\sigma^{\prime}_{i,i+1})\}_{j=1,i=1}^{j=s-1,i=s^{\prime}-1}
of
\operatorname{Symm}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{s}}\right)
. Considering the background we provided in Section
3.2
on the structure of the generators, variation is translated through the effect of the transformation
\gamma_{j,j+1;i,i+1}
. We have:

\begin{split}&\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}[\varphi]=S_{\boldsymbol{m}}[\varphi]\circ(\sigma_{k,k+1},\sigma^{\prime}_{i,i+1})-S_{\boldsymbol{m}}[\varphi]\\
&\hskip 2.84544pt=\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\bigg\{\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(1)}[\varphi]+\big(1-\frac{1}{m}\big)\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(2)}[\varphi]+\big(1-\frac{1}{m^{\prime}}\big)\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(3)}[\varphi]+\big(1+\frac{1}{m^{\prime}}\big)\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(4)}[\varphi]\bigg\}\bigg),\end{split}

(166)

where

\begin{split}&\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(1)}[\varphi]=\iint_{\tilde{O}_{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{\gamma_{j,j+1;i,i+1}w}\tilde{\varphi}|^{2}+e^{\tilde{\varphi}})\differential{(\gamma_{j,j+1;i,i+1}w)}\wedge\differential{(\overline{\gamma_{j,j+1;i,i+1}w})}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt-\iint_{O_{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{\varphi})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}},\\
&\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(2)}[\varphi]=\sum_{k=1}^{s}\bigg\{\oint_{\tilde{C}_{k}^{\epsilon}}\tilde{\varphi}\left(\frac{\differential{(\overline{\gamma_{j,j+1;i,i+1}w})}}{\overline{\gamma_{j,j+1;i,i+1}w}-\overline{\gamma_{j,j+1;i,i+1}w}_{k}}-\frac{\differential{(\gamma_{j,j+1;i,i+1}w)}}{\gamma_{j,j+1;i,i+1}w-\gamma_{j,j+1;i,i+1}w_{k}}\right)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt-\oint_{C_{k}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{k}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{k}}\right)\bigg\},\\

&\hskip 56.9055pt-\oint_{C_{k}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{k}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{k}}\right)\bigg\},\\

and
\tilde{O}_{\epsilon}
,
