69

Note that the orientation of the contour around the point at infinity is opposite of the other points, hence the sign difference in the last term.

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(1)}[\varphi]=\hskip-2.84526pt-4\pi\sqrt{-1}\left(1-\frac{1}{m}\right)^{2}\hskip-2.84526pts\log\frac{1}{|1-w_{s+s^{\prime}-3}|}-4\pi\sqrt{-1}\left(1-\frac{1}{m^{\prime}}\right)^{2}\hskip-2.84526pt(s^{\prime}-1)\log\frac{1}{|1-w_{s+s^{\prime}-3}|}

(170)

\displaystyle+4\pi\sqrt{-1}\left(1+\frac{1}{m^{\prime}}\right)^{2}\log\frac{1}{|1-w_{s+s^{\prime}-3}|}.

Next, for the
\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(2)}[\varphi]
we have

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(2)}[\varphi]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{s}\left(\oint_{\tilde{C}_{k}^{\epsilon}}\tilde{\varphi}\left(\frac{\differential{(\overline{\gamma w})}}{\overline{\gamma w}-\overline{\gamma w}_{k}}-\frac{\differential{(\gamma w)}}{\gamma w-\gamma w_{k}}\right)\right.\left.-\oint_{C_{k}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{k}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{k}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{s}\left(\oint_{\tilde{C}_{k}^{\epsilon}}(\varphi-\log|\gamma^{\prime}|^{2})\left(\frac{\bar{\gamma^{\prime}}\differential{\bar{ w}}}{\overline{\gamma w}-\overline{\gamma w}_{k}}-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}\differential{ w}}{\gamma w-\gamma w_{k}}\right)\right.\left.-\oint_{C_{k}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{k}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{k}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{s}\left(\oint_{\tilde{C}_{k}^{\epsilon}}(\varphi+2\log|1-w_{s+s^{\prime}-3}|)\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{k}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{k}}\right)\right.\left.-\oint_{C_{k}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{k}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{k}}\right)\right),

where we have used (
167
) and
\gamma^{\prime}=1/(1-w_{s+s^{\prime}-3})
. By using the asymptotics of
\varphi
given in Lemma
C.1
and equation (
169
), the above expression can be simplified to give

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(2)}[\varphi]=-8\pi\sqrt{-1}\hskip 2.84526pt\frac{1}{m}s\log\frac{1}{|1-w_{s+s^{\prime}-3}|}.

(171)

By identical calculation to that of
\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(2)}[\varphi]
, we get:

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(3)}[\varphi]=-8\pi\sqrt{-1}\hskip 2.84526pt\frac{1}{m^{\prime}}(s^{\prime}-1)\log\frac{1}{|1-w_{s+s^{\prime}-3}|}.

(172)

Finally,

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(4)}[\varphi]

\displaystyle=\oint_{\tilde{C}_{s+s^{\prime}}^{\epsilon}}\tilde{\varphi}\left(\frac{\differential{(\overline{\gamma w})}}{\overline{\gamma w}}-\frac{\differential{(\gamma w)}}{\gamma w}\right)-\oint_{C_{s+s^{\prime}}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w}\right)

\displaystyle=\oint_{\tilde{C}_{s+s^{\prime}}^{\epsilon}}(\varphi-\log|\gamma^{\prime}|^{2})\left(\frac{\bar{\gamma^{\prime}}\differential{ \bar{ w}}}{\overline{\gamma w}}-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}\differential{w}}{\gamma w}\right)-\oint_{C_{s+s^{\prime}}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w}\right)

\displaystyle=\oint_{\tilde{C}_{s+s^{\prime}}^{\epsilon}}(\varphi+2\log|1-w_{s+s^{\prime}-3}|)\left(\frac{\differential{ \bar{ w}}}{\bar{w}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w}\right)-\oint_{C_{s+s^{\prime}}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w}\right),

where in the last line we approximated
w-w_{s+s^{\prime}-3}
with
w
in the denominator of the second term due to
w
having a large absolute value. Again, using the asymptotics of
\varphi
from Lemma
C.1
, Eq.(
169
) and taking into account the opposite orientation of contours around the point at infinity, we have:

\displaystyle\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{(4)}[\varphi]=-8\pi\sqrt{-1}\hskip 2.84526pt\frac{1}{m^{\prime}}\log\frac{1}{|1-w_{s+s^{\prime}-3}|}.

(173)

Putting (
166
), (
170
), (
171
), (
172
) and (
173
) together, we get

\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}[\varphi]=2\pi\left(\frac{1}{m^{2}}-1\right)s\log|1-w_{s+s^{\prime}-3}|+2\pi\left(\frac{1}{m^{\prime 2}}-1\right)(s^{\prime}-2)\log|1-w_{s+s^{\prime}-3}|.

(174)

By comparing (
174
) with equation (
65
), we can readily see that for
i=s^{\prime}-3
:

\Delta S_{\boldsymbol{m}}[\varphi]=S_{\boldsymbol{m}}[\varphi]\circ(\sigma_{k,k+1},\sigma^{\prime}_{i,i+1})-S_{\boldsymbol{m}}[\varphi]=-2\pi\log|f_{(\sigma_{k,k+1},\sigma^{\prime}_{i,i+1})}|.

(175)

Doing the analogous calculations for
i=s^{\prime}-2,s^{\prime}-3
would yield similar results. One can continue this proof for the case with the direct product of the symmetric groups of more strata inductively. Furthermore, had we chosen to fix the points
0,1
and
\infty
in the other stratum or decided to deal with punctures, we would still get the same result with minor changes in the path of the proof. So, in principle, we proved that under the action of any element
\eta
of
\operatorname{Symm}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{s}}\right)
,
S_{\boldsymbol{m}}
transforms according to the rule

\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}\circ\eta/\pi]\hskip 2.84526pt|f_{\eta}|^{2}=\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/\pi].

(176)

This means that
\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/\pi]
is a Hermitian metric in the holomorphic
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
\lambda_{0,\boldsymbol{m}}
defined in the Section
3.2
. As we mentioned before, (
175
) shows that 1-cocycles can be viewed as the modular anomaly caused by the non-covariance of the action under the effect of the modular group. To complete the proof, we remind that the first Chern form
\mathsf{c}_{1}\left(\lambda_{0,\boldsymbol{m}},\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/\pi]\right)
of the metrized
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
\lambda_{0,\boldsymbol{m}}
with a metric
\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/\pi]
is given by

\mathsf{c}_{1}\left(\lambda_{0,\boldsymbol{m}},\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/\pi]\right)=-\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\hskip 2.84526pt\partial\bar{\partial}\left(\frac{S_{\boldsymbol{m}}}{\pi}\right).

(177)

Thus using Theorem
159
, we find that

\mathsf{c}_{1}\left(\lambda_{0,\boldsymbol{m}},\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/\pi]\right)=\frac{1}{\pi^{2}}\omega_{\text{WP}}.

(178)

This statement completes the proof.
∎

4.2
Riemann Orbisurfaces of Genus >1

Consider a marked normalized Schottky group of rank
g>1
denoted by
(\Sigma;L_{1},\dots,L_{g})
. The Liouville action, at the classical level, is actually the on-shell value of the Liouville action functional. For the case of a closed Riemann surface with
g>1
it was first defined by Zograf and Takhtajan
1988SbMat..60..297Z
(and was later interpreted by Takhtajan and Teo
Takhtajan:2002cc
in cohomological language) to be given by

S[\varphi]=\iint_{\mathcal{D}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{\varphi})\differential[2]{w}+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\theta_{L_{k}^{-1}}(\varphi),

(179)

where the 1-form
\theta_{L_{k}^{-1}}(\varphi)
is given by

\theta_{L_{k}^{-1}}(\varphi)=\left(\varphi-\frac{1}{2}\log|L_{k}^{\prime}|^{2}-\log|l_{k}|^{2}\right)\left(\frac{L_{k}^{\prime\prime}}{L_{k}^{\prime}}\differential{w}-\frac{\overline{L_{k}^{\prime\prime}}}{\overline{L_{k}^{\prime}}}\differential{\bar{w}}\right),\qquad\forall\hskip 1.42262ptL_{k}\in(\Sigma;L_{1},\dots,L_{g}).

(180)

In the above equations,
\mathcal{D}
is the fundamental domain of the marked normalized Schottky group
(\Sigma;L_{1},\dots,L_{g})
,
\partial\mathcal{D}=\bigcup_{k=1}^{g}(C_{k}\cup C_{k}^{\prime})
, and

l_{k}=\frac{1-\lambda_{k}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}(a_{k}-b_{k})}

(181)

is the left-hand lower element in the matrix representation of the generator
L_{k}\in\operatorname{PSL}(2,\mathbb{C})
for
k=2,\dots,g
. Notice that since we have chosen the marked Schottky group to be normalized, in particular
a_{1}=0
and
b_{1}=\infty
, we have
l_{1}=0
and
\theta_{L_{1}^{-1}}(\varphi)=0
. Zograf and Takhtajan
(
1988SbMat..60..297Z
, Theorems 1,2)
have proven that

\partial S=-2\pi\sum_{i=1}^{3g-3}c_{i}P_{i}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bar{\partial}\partial S=-2\sqrt{-1}\omega_{WP},

(182)

where
\partial
and
\bar{\partial}
are
(1,0)
and
(0,1)
components of the de Rham differential on
\mathfrak{S}_{g}
(see Section
3.3
).
This implies that
-S
is actually a Kähler potential for the projection of Weil-Petersson metric on
\mathfrak{S}_{g}
.

Remark 4.5
.

The addition of the second term in the on-shell Liouville action (
179
) makes sure that:

•

The variation
\delta S
of the classical action
S[\varphi]
has the form

\delta S[\varphi]\overset{\text{\tiny\it def.}}{\equiv}\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{S[\varphi+t\delta\psi]-S[\varphi]}{t}=\iint_{\mathcal{D}}(-2\partial_{\bar{w}}\partial_{w}\varphi+e^{\varphi})\delta\psi\differential[2]{w},

(183)

where the variation
\delta\psi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathbb{R})
is a smooth function on
\Omega
which is automorphic with respect to
\Sigma
.
For the marked Schottky group
(\Sigma;L_{1},\dots,L_{g})
, the
S[\varphi]
is
independent
of the specific choice of a fundamental domain
\mathcal{D}
.
70
70

70

See footnote
51
.

When the conical points and cusps are present, the area integral in (
179
) diverges in the limits
w\to w_{i}
due to asymptotics of Liouville field
\varphi
(see Lemma
C.1
) and the classical Liouville action needs to be regularized. Let us define
\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}
as the pair
(\mathcal{D}_{0},\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}|_{{}_{\mathcal{D}}})
where
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is defined as
\mathcal{D}\cap\Omega_{0}
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}|_{{}_{\mathcal{D}}}
denotes the restriction of
\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}
to
\mathcal{D}
. Here, we have assumed that all singular points
w_{1},\dots,w_{n}
belong to the interior of fundamental domain
\mathcal{D}
. For sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, define
71
71

71

Note that
\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}=\mathcal{D}_{\text{reg}}
where
\mathcal{D}_{\text{reg}}:=\mathcal{D}_{0}\backslash\operatorname{Supp}(\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}|_{{}_{\mathcal{D}}})
.

\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}=\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}\big\backslash\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}^{\epsilon},

(184)

with
D_{i}^{\epsilon}\overset{\text{\tiny\it def.}}{\equiv}\left\{w\,\big|\,|w-w_{i}|<\epsilon\right\}
. It follows from Lemma
C.1
that the following limit exists

S_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\text{reg}}}[\varphi]=\\
\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\left(\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{2\varphi})\differential[2]{w}+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)\right.\\
\left.-2\pi\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)^{2}\log\epsilon+2\pi n_{p}\big(\log\epsilon+2\log|\log\epsilon|\big)\right).

(185)

Remark 4.6
.

When
n_{p}=0
, the appropriate
S_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\text{reg}}}[\varphi]
is given by

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0^{+}}\Bigg(\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{2\varphi})\differential[2]{w}+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)\\
-2\pi\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)^{2}\log\epsilon\Bigg).

(186)

Now, we can define the regularized action as

\begin{split}&S_{\boldsymbol{m}}[\varphi]=S_{\boldsymbol{m}}(\mathcal{D};w_{1},\dots,w_{n})=S_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\text{reg}}}[\varphi]+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\theta_{L_{k}^{-1}}(\varphi).\end{split}

(187)

This completes the definition of
S_{\boldsymbol{m}}
provided that all of the fixed points
w_{1},\dots,w_{n}
lie in the interior of the fundamental domain — i.e.
w_{1},\dots,w_{n}\in\operatorname{Int}\mathcal{D}
. The
S_{\boldsymbol{m}}(\mathcal{D};w_{1},\dots,w_{n})
depends on the choice of representatives in
\Sigma\cdot\{w_{1},\dots,w_{n}\}
and
no longer determines a function on the Schottky space
\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
. Note that
w_{i}
and
L_{k}(w_{i})
have the same order and are related by the action of
\operatorname{Symm}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{s}}\right)
acting on
\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\Omega}
. The geometric meaning of
S_{\boldsymbol{m}}
is given by the following Lemma (also see Lemma
3.5
):

Lemma 4.2
.

The regularized Liouville action determines a Hermitian metric
\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/\pi]
in the holomorphic
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
\mathscr{L}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{L}_{i}^{h_{i}}
over
\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
.

Proof.

To establish this claim for
i=1,\dots,n
, it is adequate to demonstrate that we have

S_{\boldsymbol{m}}(\tilde{\mathcal{D}};w_{1},\dots,L_{k}w_{i},\dots,w_{n})-S_{\boldsymbol{m}}(\mathcal{D};w_{1},\dots,w_{n})=\pi h_{i}\hskip 1.42262pt\log|L_{k}^{\prime}(w_{i})|^{2},

(188)

where
w_{1},\dots,w_{n}\in\operatorname{Int}\mathcal{D}
and
w_{1},\dots,L_{k}w_{i},\dots,w_{n}\in\operatorname{Int}\tilde{\mathcal{D}}
. Furthermore, it is sufficient to consider the case when

\tilde{\mathcal{D}}=(\mathcal{D}\backslash\mathcal{D}_{0})\cup L_{k}(\mathcal{D}_{0}),

and
\mathcal{D}_{0}\subset\mathcal{D}
is such that
\partial\mathcal{D}_{0}\cap\partial\mathcal{D}\subset C_{k}
and
w_{i}\in\mathcal{D}_{0}
, and all other
w_{j}\in\mathcal{D}\backslash\mathcal{D}_{0}
for
j\neq i
. Note that by a finite combination of such transformations, any choice of a fundamental domain for
\Sigma
can be obtained from
\mathcal{D}
. The computation of (
188
) closely follows the corresponding computation in the proof of Lemma 3 in
park2015potentials
where no branch point were present. Let

\begin{split}I_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{D};w_{1},\dots,w_{n})&=\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{\varphi})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}+\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\theta_{L_{k}^{-1}}(\varphi)\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right),\end{split}

where we did not include the counterterms in the action due to the fact that the action of
L_{k}
does not change the conformal class. Since
\tilde{C}_{j}=C_{j}
for
j\neq k
and
\tilde{C}_{k}=C_{k}-\partial\mathcal{D}_{0}
, we have

\begin{split}\Delta I_{\epsilon}&=I_{\epsilon}(\tilde{\mathcal{D}};w_{1},\dots,L_{k}w_{i},\dots,w_{n})-I_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{D};w_{1},\dots,w_{n})\\
&=\iint_{L_{k}(\mathcal{D}_{0})\backslash\tilde{D}_{i}^{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{\varphi})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}-\iint_{\mathcal{D}_{0}\backslash D_{i}^{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{\varphi})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}-\oint_{\partial\mathcal{D}_{0}}\theta_{L_{k}^{-1}}(\varphi)\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\delta_{i\,j}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\left[\oint_{\tilde{C}_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\overline{L_{k}w_{j}}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-L_{k}w_{j}}\right)-\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)\right].\end{split}

According to Eq.(
132
) we have

\begin{split}L_{k}^{\ast}\left((|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{\varphi})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\right)&=\left((|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{\varphi})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\right)\circ L_{k}\,|L_{k}^{\prime}|^{2}\\
&=(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{\varphi})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}+\differential{\theta_{L_k^{-1}}(\varphi)},\end{split}

(189)

which, together with using the Stokes theorem, we get

\begin{split}\Delta I_{\epsilon}&=\iint_{\mathcal{D}_{0}\backslash L_{k}^{-1}(\tilde{D}_{i}^{\epsilon})}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{\varphi})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}-\iint_{\mathcal{D}_{0}\backslash D_{i}^{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}+e^{\varphi})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}-\oint_{\partial L_{k}^{-1}(\tilde{D}_{i}^{\epsilon})}\theta_{L_{k}^{-1}}(\varphi)\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\delta_{i\,j}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\left[\oint_{L_{k}^{-1}(\tilde{C}_{j}^{\epsilon})}(\varphi-\log|L^{\prime}_{k}w|^{2})\left(\frac{\overline{L^{\prime}_{k}w}\differential{\bar{w}}}{\overline{L_{k}w}-\overline{L_{k}w_{j}}}-\frac{L^{\prime}_{k}w\differential{w}}{L_{k}w-L_{k}w_{j}}\right)\right.\\
&\left.\hskip 56.9055pt-\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)\right].\end{split}

Since the third term’s integrand does not have a pole, its contribution will be of
\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)
and it can be safely omitted. Furthermore, the exponential term in the Liouville term gives the Euler characteristic and its contribution will be canceled between related terms. Now, we can rewrite the above equation as

\begin{split}\Delta I_{\epsilon}&=\iint_{D_{i}^{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}-\iint_{L_{k}^{-1}(\tilde{D}_{i}^{\epsilon})}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\delta_{i\,j}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\left[\oint_{L_{k}^{-1}(\tilde{C}_{j}^{\epsilon})}(\varphi-\log|L^{\prime}_{k}(w)|^{2})\left(\partial_{\bar{w}}\log|L_{k}w-L_{k}w_{j}|^{2}\right.\right.\\
&\left.\left.-\partial_{w}\log|L_{k}w-L_{k}w_{j}|^{2}\right)-\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)\right].\end{split}

(190)

By noting that

\displaystyle\partial_{w}\log|L_{k}w-L_{k}w_{j}|^{2}=\partial_{w}\log|w-w_{j}|^{2}+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right),

we can write (
190
) as follows

\begin{split}\Delta I_{\epsilon}&=\iint_{D_{i}^{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}-\iint_{L_{k}^{-1}(\tilde{D}_{i}^{\epsilon})}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\delta_{i\,j}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\left[\oint_{L_{k}^{-1}(\tilde{C}_{j}^{\epsilon})}(\varphi-\log|L^{\prime}_{k}(w)|^{2})\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)\right.\\
&\left.\hskip 56.9055pt-\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)\right].\end{split}

(191)

By doing the integration by parts and imposing the equations of motion together with considering the orientation of the boundary circles, the first line in (
191
) becomes

\displaystyle\iint_{D_{i}^{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}-\iint_{L_{k}^{-1}(\tilde{D}_{i}^{\epsilon})}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}=\oint_{C_{i}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\differential\bar{w}-\oint_{\tilde{C}_{i}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\differential\bar{w},

(192)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{C}_{i}^{\epsilon}=\{w\big|\>|L_{k}w-L_{k}w_{i}|=\epsilon\}\equiv\{w\big|\>|w-w_{i}|=\frac{\epsilon}{L^{\prime}_{k}w_{i}}\}.

(193)

To be more precise, the right hand side of (
192
) had an extra contribution

\frac{1}{2}\iint_{L_{k}^{-1}(\tilde{D}_{i}^{\epsilon})}\varphi e^{\varphi}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}-\frac{1}{2}\iint_{D_{i}^{\epsilon}}\varphi e^{\varphi}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}},

(194)

which by defining
y_{i}=|w-w_{i}|,\hskip 1.42262pt\alpha_{i}=1-1/m_{i},[y_{i}]=:\epsilon\leq y_{i}\leq\epsilon/\penalty L^{\prime}_{k}w_{i}
, this contribution for branch points and cusps respectively becomes

\begin{split}&-\frac{2\pi}{2}\iint_{[y_{i}]}\frac{1}{y_{i}^{2\alpha_{i}-1}}\log{y_i^{2\alpha_{i}}}dy_{i}=\pi\left(\frac{\alpha_{i}}{2(1-\alpha_{i})^{2}}y_{i}^{2-2\alpha_{i}}-\frac{\alpha_{i}}{(1-\alpha_{i})}y_{i}^{2-2\alpha_{i}}\log y_{i}\right)\bigg|^{\epsilon/\penalty L^{\prime}_{k}w_{i}}_{\epsilon}\\
&-\hskip-1.42262pt\frac{2\pi}{2}\hskip-2.84526pt\iint_{[y_{i}]}\frac{1}{y_{i}\log^{2}y_{i}}\log\left({y_{i}^{2}}\log^{2}y_{i}\right)\hskip-1.42262ptdy_{i}\hskip-1.42262pt=\hskip-1.42262pt\pi\hskip-1.42262pt\left(2\hskip-1.42262pt+\hskip-1.42262pt\frac{2}{\log y_{i}}\hskip-1.42262pt-\hskip-1.42262pt2\log|\log y_{i}|\hskip-1.42262pt+\hskip-1.42262pt\frac{2}{\log y_{i}}\log|\log y_{i}|\right)\hskip-2.84526pt\bigg|^{\epsilon/\penalty L^{\prime}_{k}w_{i}}_{\epsilon}\end{split}

but both of them, and accordingly (
194
), vanish in the limit
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
. Now, by using the asymptotic form of
\varphi
from the Lemma
C.1
together with the relation (
193
), the Eq.(
192
) is simplified to
72
72

72

The
D_{i}^{\epsilon}
’s boundaries have the opposite orientation of the boundary of the fundamental domain.

\displaystyle\iint_{D_{i}^{\epsilon}}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}-\iint_{L_{k}^{-1}(\tilde{D}_{i}^{\epsilon})}(|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}=-4\pi\sqrt{-1}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)^{2}\log|L^{\prime}_{k}w_{i}|.

(195)

For the rest of the integrals in (
191
), again by using the asymptotics of
\varphi
from Lemma
C.1
and (
193
) we have

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\delta_{ij}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\left[\oint_{L_{k}^{-1}(\tilde{C}_{j}^{\epsilon})}(\varphi-\log|L^{\prime}_{k}(w)|^{2})\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)\right.

(196)

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 36.98866pt-\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\varphi\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}}\right)\right]=8\pi\sqrt{-1}\left(\frac{1}{m_{i}^{2}}-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)\log|L^{\prime}_{k}w_{i}|.

Now, combining (
195
) and (
196
) gives

\Delta I_{\epsilon}=-4\pi\sqrt{-1}\hskip 2.84526pth_{i}\log|L^{\prime}_{k}w_{i}|.

So, in principle, we proved that

\displaystyle\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}(\tilde{\mathcal{D}};w_{1},\dots,L_{k}w_{i},\dots,w_{n})/(\pi h_{i})]=\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}(\mathcal{D};w_{1},\dots,w_{n})/(\pi h_{i})]\hskip 2.84526pt|L^{\prime}_{k}w_{i}|^{2},

which means that
\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/\pi]
is a Hermitian metric on
\mathscr{L}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n_{p}+n_{e}}\mathscr{L}_{i}^{h_{i}}
.
∎

Combining Lemmas
4.2
and
3.5
, we can deduce the following statement:

Corollary 4.1
.

Put
\mathsf{H}=\mathsf{h}_{1}^{m_{1}h_{1}}\dotsm\mathsf{h}_{n_{e}}^{m_{n_{e}}h_{n_{e}}}\mathsf{h}_{n_{e}+1}\dotsm\mathsf{h}_{n}
. Then,

\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}=S_{\boldsymbol{m}}-\pi\log\mathsf{H}

(197)

determines a smooth real-valued function on
\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
.

The above form of
\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}
can be easily understand by demanding the Liouville action to be independent from the choice of a fundamental domain: It is clear from commutative diagram (
13
) and the definition of regularized Liouville action (
187
), that the problem of defining the appropriate Liouville action on
\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
is closely related to the Fuchsian uniformization of
\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\Omega}
. Then, the fact that
\Omega\subset\hat{\mathbb{C}}
and the observation that, roughly speaking, the action of Schottky group
\Sigma
on
\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\Omega}
resembles that of
\operatorname{Symm}\left(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{s}}\right)
on a genus zero Riemann orbisurface,
73
73

73

More specifically, by acting each generator
L_{k}\in\Sigma
on a singular point
w_{i}
inside a particular fundamental domain
\mathcal{D}
, we will get another singular point with same order of isotropy in a different fundamental domain.
suggests that the same pattern of “anomaly cancellation” observed in section
3.2.1
should also happen in this case.

5
Potentials for Weil-Petersson and Takhtajan-Zograf Metrics

In this section, following
park2015potentials
;
ZT_2018
, we construct Kähler potentials for cuspidal and elliptic TZ metrics on
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
(see Section
3.1.3
). We will also prove that the first Chern forms of the line bundles
\mathscr{L}_{i}
over the Schottky space
\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
with Hermitian metrics
\mathsf{h}_{i}
are given by
\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\omega^{\text{ell}}_{\text{TZ},i}
for
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
and
\tfrac{4}{3}\omega^{\text{cusp}}_{\text{TZ},i}
for
i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n
. In addition, we will show that
\tfrac{1}{\pi^{2}}\omega_{{}_{\text{WP}}}
is the first Chern form of the
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
\mathscr{L}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{L}_{i}^{h_{i}}
with Hermitian metric
\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/\pi]
, where the regularized classical Liouville action
S_{\boldsymbol{m}}
is given by Eq.(
187
). Then, it follows readily from these two results that the specific combination
\omega_{{}_{\text{WP}}}-\tfrac{4\pi^{2}}{3}\omega_{{}_{\text{TZ}}}^{\text{cusp}}-\tfrac{\pi}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}m_{j}h_{j}\hskip 1.42262pt\omega^{\text{ell}}_{\text{TZ},j}
of Weil-Petersson metric as well as cuspidal and elliptic Takhtajan-Zograf metrics has a
global
Kähler potential on
\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
.

5.1
Potentials for Cuspidal and Elliptic TZ Metrics on
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}

As in Section
3.2
, let
\Gamma
be a marked normalized Fuchsian group with signature
(0;m_{1},\dots,m_{n_{e}}

,n_{p})
that uniformizes the orbifold Riemann surface
O
and let
J:\mathbb{H}\to O
be the Klien’s Hauptmodule. In addition, let
\mathsf{h}_{i}=\left|J^{(i)}_{1}\right|^{\frac{2}{m_{i}}}
for
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
,
\mathsf{h}_{i}=\left|J^{(i)}_{1}\right|^{2}
for
i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
, and
\mathsf{h}_{n}=\left|J^{(n)}_{-1}\right|^{2}
be smooth positive functions on
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
.
74
74

74

See Eq.(
61
).
Now, according to the expressions for
\log\mathsf{h}_{i}
in Remark
3.10
, we prove the following lemma:

Lemma 5.1
.

For all
k=1,\dots,n-3
, we have

\begin{split}&\partialderivative{w_k}\log\mathsf{h}_{i}=\frac{1}{m_{i}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{k}(w_{i}),\hskip 28.45274pti=1,\dots,n_{e},\\
\\
&\partialderivative{w_k}\log\mathsf{h}_{i}=\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{k}(w_{i}),\hskip 42.67912pti=n_{e}+1,\dots,n.\end{split}

(198)

Proof.

Consider the orbifold Riemann surface
O\cong[\mathbb{H}/\penalty\Gamma]
. Using Lemma
3.3
, it is sufficient to demonstrate that

\left.\left(\partialderivative{\log\Lponetial_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_k}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\left\{\begin{split}&\frac{1}{m_{i}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{k}(w_{i})&\text{for}\quad&i=1,\dots,n_{e},\\
\\
&\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{k}(w_{i})\quad&\text{for}\quad&i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n,\end{split}\right.

(199)

and for all
k=1,\dots,n-3
. The following proof repeats verbatim the proof of Lemma 4 in
park2015potentials
for the case of punctures. Using the fact that
F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}
is holomorphic in
\varepsilon
at
\varepsilon=0
, Corollary
3.1
and formulas in Remark
3.10
, Eq.(
106
) we get:

•

i=1,\dots,n_{e}
case:

\begin{split}\hskip-14.22636pt\left.\left(\partialderivative{\log\Lponetial_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_k}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}&=-\left.\left(\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\ \left(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}+\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)\log|F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w-w_{i})|^{2}\right)\\
&\hskip-28.45274pt=-\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\left\{\left.\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\left(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}+\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)\log|F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w-w_{i})|^{2}\right)\right\}\\
&\hskip-28.45274pt=-\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\left(\partial_{w_{k}}\varphi+\partial_{w}\varphi\dot{F}^{k}(w)+\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)\frac{\dot{F}^{k}(w)-\dot{F}^{k}(w_{i})}{w-w_{i}}\right)\\
&\hskip-34.14322pt\overset{\eqref{widerivative}}{=}-\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\left(-\partial_{w}\varphi\dot{F}^{k}(w)-\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{k}(w)+\partial_{w}\varphi\dot{F}^{k}(w)+\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{k}(w)\right)\\
&\hskip-28.45274pt=\frac{1}{m_{i}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{k}(w_{i}).\end{split}

In going from the first line to the second line, we have interchanged the order of the limit
w\to w_{i}
and the differentiation. This is allowed due to convergence in the above formula and the fact that the definition of
\mathsf{h}_{i}
is uniform in a neighborhood of an arbitrary point
(w_{1},\dots,w_{n-3})\in\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
.

•

i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
case:

\begin{split}\hskip-11.38092pt\left.\left(\partialderivative{\log\Lponetial_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_k}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}&=\left.\left(\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\left(\log|F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w-w_{i})|^{2}-\frac{2e^{-\frac{\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w)}{2}}}{|F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w-w_{i})|}\right)\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt=\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\left\{\left.\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\left(\log|F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w-w_{i})|^{2}-2(F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}})^{\ast}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}}\right)\left|\frac{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w)}{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w-w_{i})}\right|\right)\right\}\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt=\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\left\{\left.\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\log(F^{\varepsilon\mu_k}(w-w_i))-2\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varphi}}{(\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{i})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left.\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\left(\frac{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w)}{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w-w_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt=\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\left(\frac{\dot{F}^{k}(w)-\dot{F}^{k}(w_{i})}{w-w_{i}}-\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varphi(w)}\left((w-w_{i})\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{k}(w)-\dot{F}^{k}(w)+\dot{F}^{k}(w_{i})\right)}{(w-w_{i})|w-w_{i}|}\right)\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt=\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{k}(w_{i}).\end{split}

•

i=n
case:

\begin{split}\hskip-8.5359pt\left.\left(\partialderivative{\log\Lponetial_{n}^{\varepsilon\mu_k}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}&=\left.\left(\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\lim_{w\to\infty}\left(\log|F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w)|^{2}-\frac{2e^{-\frac{\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w)}{2}}}{|F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w)|}\right)\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt=\lim_{w\to\infty}\left\{\left.\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\left(\log|F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}|^{2}-2(F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}})^{\ast}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}}\right)\left|\frac{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}}{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}}\right|\right)(w)\right\}\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt=\lim_{w\to\infty}\left\{\left.\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\log(F^{\varepsilon\mu_k}(w)\overline{F^{\varepsilon\mu_k}(w)})-2\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varphi}}{\bar{w}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left.\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\left(\frac{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w)}{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{k}}(w)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt=\lim_{w\to\infty}\left(\frac{\dot{F}^{k}(w)}{w}-\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varphi(w)}(w\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{k}(w)-\dot{F}^{k}(w))|w|}{w^{2}\bar{w}}\right)\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt=\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{k}(\infty).\end{split}

∎

As before, let
\partial
and
\bar{\partial}
be the
(1,0)
and
(0,1)
components of de Rham differential
d=\partial+\bar{\partial}
on
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
. We have (see
park2015potentials
;
ZT_2018
):

Lemma

(Takhtajan and Zograf)
.

The functions
-\log\mathsf{h}_{i},-\log\mathsf{h}_{j},\log\mathsf{h}_{n},:\mathcal{M}_{0,n}\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
for
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
and
j=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
are Kähler potential for TZ metrics
\frac{1}{2}\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\text{TZ},i}^{\text{ell}}
,
\frac{4\pi}{3}\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\text{TZ},j}^{\text{cusp}}
,
-\frac{4\pi}{3}\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\text{TZ},n}^{\text{cusp}}
, respectively:
75
75

75

See the Proposition 1 in
park2015potentials
for punctured Riemann surfaces.

\bar{\partial}\partial\log\mathsf{h}_{i}=-\sqrt{-1}\hskip 1.42262pt\omega_{\text{TZ},i}^{\text{ell}},\qquad\bar{\partial}\partial\log\mathsf{h}_{j}=-\frac{8\pi\sqrt{-1}}{3}\hskip 1.42262pt\omega_{\text{TZ},j}^{\text{cusp}},\qquad\bar{\partial}\partial\log\mathsf{h}_{n}=\frac{8\pi\sqrt{-1}}{3}\hskip 1.42262pt\omega_{\text{TZ},n}^{\text{cusp}}.

(200)

Proof.

We need to prove that for all
j,k=1,\dots,n-3
,

-\partialderivative{\log\Lponetial_{i}}{w_j}{\bar{w}_k}=\left\{\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\partialderivative{w_j},\partialderivative{w_k}\right\rangle_{\text{TZ},i}^{\text{ell}}&\text{for}\quad&i=1,\dots,n_{e},\\
\\
&\frac{4\pi}{3}\left\langle\partialderivative{w_j},\partialderivative{w_k}\right\rangle_{\text{TZ},i}^{\text{cusp}}&\text{for}\quad&i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1,\\
\\
&-\frac{4\pi}{3}\left\langle\partialderivative{w_j},\partialderivative{w_k}\right\rangle_{\text{TZ},i}^{\text{cusp}}\quad&\text{for}\quad&i=n.\end{split}\right.

Let us consider the three cases
i=1,...,n_{e}
,
i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
, and
i=n
separately. The following proof repeats verbatim the proof of Proposition 1 in
park2015potentials
for the case of punctures. According to Section
3.1
, Lemma
3.3
and Eq.(
106
), for a given Riemann orbisurface
O\cong[\mathbb{H}/\penalty\Gamma]
one can write:

•

i=1,\dots,n_{e}
case:

\begin{split}\hskip-14.22636pt-\partialderivative{\log\Lponetial_{i}}{w_j}{\bar{w}_k}&=-\left.\left(\partialderivative{\log\Lponetial_{i}^{\varepsilon_j \mu_j + \varepsilon_k \mu_k}}{\varepsilon_j}{\bar{\varepsilon}_k}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon_{j}=\varepsilon_{k}=0}\\
&\hskip-45.52458pt=\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\left\{\left.\left(\partialderivative{}{\varepsilon_j}{\bar{\varepsilon}_k}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon_{j}=\varepsilon_{k}=0}\left(\varphi^{(\varepsilon\mu)_{jk}}\circ F^{(\varepsilon\mu)_{jk}}+\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}\right)\log|F^{(\varepsilon\mu)_{jk}}(w-w_{i})|^{2}\right)\right\}\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt=\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\left.\left(\partialderivative{}{\varepsilon_j}{\bar{\varepsilon}_k}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon_{j}=\varepsilon_{k}=0}\left(F^{\varepsilon_{j}\mu_{j}+\varepsilon_{k}\mu_{k}}\right)^{\ast}(\varphi).\end{split}

In the above equation, we have used the notation
(\varepsilon\mu)_{jk}=\varepsilon_{j}\mu_{j}+\varepsilon_{k}\mu_{k}
. Using the commutative diagram (
84
), one has

(F^{\varepsilon_{j}\mu_{j}+\varepsilon_{k}\mu_{k}})^{\ast}\Big(e^{\varphi^{\varepsilon_{j}\mu_{j}+\varepsilon_{k}\mu_{k}}}\Big)=(J^{-1})^{\ast}(f^{\varepsilon_{j}\mu_{j}+\varepsilon_{k}\mu_{k}})^{\ast}(\rho).

Taking the logarithm of the above formula, we get

\varphi^{\varepsilon_{j}\mu_{j}+\varepsilon_{k}\mu_{k}}\circ F^{\varepsilon_{j}\mu_{j}+\varepsilon_{k}\mu_{k}}+\log|\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon_{j}\mu_{j}+\varepsilon_{k}\mu_{k}}|^{2}=\log((f^{\varepsilon_j \mu_j + \varepsilon_k \mu_k})^{\ast}(\rho))\circ J^{-1}+\log|(J^{-1})^{\prime}|^{2}.

Then, using the above equations together with Ahlfors formulae (
101
) and Wolpert’s formula (
45
), we have

\begin{split}-\left.\left(\partialderivative{\log\Lponetial_{i}^{\varepsilon_j \mu_j + \varepsilon_k \mu_k}}{\varepsilon_j}{\bar{\varepsilon}_k}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon_{j}=\varepsilon_{k}=0}&=\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\left.\left(\partialderivative{}{\varepsilon_j}{\bar{\varepsilon}_k}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon_{j}=\varepsilon_{k}=0}\log((f^{\varepsilon_j \mu_j + \varepsilon_k \mu_k})^{\ast}(\rho))\circ J^{-1}\\
&\overset{\eqref{WolpertSecVar}}{=}\frac{1}{2}\lim_{w\to w_{i}}f_{\mu_{j}\bar{\mu}_{k}}\circ J^{-1}(w)=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\partialderivative{w_j},\partialderivative{w_k}\right\rangle_{\text{TZ},i}^{\text{ell}}.\end{split}

•

i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
case:

\begin{split}\hskip-11.38092pt-\partialderivative{\log\Lponetial_{i}}{w_j}{\bar{w}_k}&=-\left.\left(\partialderivative{\log\Lponetial_{i}^{\varepsilon_j \mu_j + \varepsilon_k \mu_k}}{\varepsilon_j}{\bar{\varepsilon}_k}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon_{j}=\varepsilon_{k}=0}\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt=2\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\left\{\frac{1}{|w-w_{i}|}\left.\partialderivative{}{\varepsilon_j}{\bar{\varepsilon}_k}\right|_{\varepsilon_{j}=\varepsilon_{k}=0}\left(F^{(\varepsilon\mu)_{jk}}\right)^{\ast}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varphi^{(\varepsilon\mu)_{jk}}(w)}\right)\right.\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\left.e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varphi(w)}\left.\partialderivative{\varepsilon_j}\right|_{\varepsilon_{j}=0}\left(\frac{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon_{j}\mu_{j}}(w)}{F^{\varepsilon_{j}\mu_{j}}(w-w_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left.\partialderivative{\bar{\varepsilon}_k}\right|_{\varepsilon_{k}=0}\left(\frac{\overline{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon_{k}\mu_{k}}(w)}}{\overline{F^{\varepsilon_{k}\mu_{k}}(w-w_{i})}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\\
&\hskip-45.52458pt\overset{\eqref{expphivariations}}{=}\lim_{w\to w_{i}}\left\{-\frac{1}{2}\log|w-w_{i}|f_{\mu_{j}\bar{\mu}_{k}}\circ J^{-1}(w)+\frac{1}{2}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varphi(w)}\,\times\right.\\
&\left.\frac{\left((w-w_{i})\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{j}(w)-\dot{F}^{j}(w)+\dot{F}^{j}(w_{i})\right)}{|w-w_{i}|(\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{i})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{\left(\overline{(w-w_{i})\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{k}(w)-\dot{F}^{k}(w)+\dot{F}^{k}(w_{i})}\right)}{|w-w_{i}|(w-w_{i})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\}\\
&\hskip-45.52458pt\overset{\eqref{diagJexpansion}}{=}-\frac{1}{2}\lim_{w\to\infty}\frac{\log|w-w_{i}|}{\imaginary(\varsigma_{i}^{-1}\circ J^{-1}(w))}(\imaginary\varsigma_{i}z)f_{\mu_{j}\bar{\mu}_{k}}(\varsigma_{i}z)\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt=\pi\lim_{w\to\infty}(\imaginary\varsigma_{i}z)f_{\mu_{j}\bar{\mu}_{k}}(\varsigma_{i}z)\overset{\eqref{constcuspmetric}}{=}\frac{4\pi}{3}\left\langle\partialderivative{w_j},\partialderivative{w_k}\right\rangle_{TZ,i}^{cusp},\end{split}

where in the last line, we have used the fact that
\log|w-w_{i}|/\imaginary(\varsigma_{i}^{-1}\circ J^{-1}(w))=-2\pi
as
w\to w_{i}
.

•

i=n
case:

\begin{split}\hskip-11.38092pt\partialderivative{\log\Lponetial_{1}}{w_j}{\bar{w}_k}&=\left.\left(\partialderivative{\log\Lponetial_{1}^{\varepsilon_j \mu_j + \varepsilon_k \mu_k}}{\varepsilon_j}{\bar{\varepsilon}_k}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon_{j}=\varepsilon_{k}=0}\\
&\hskip-36.98866pt=\lim_{w\to\infty}\left\{\left.\partialderivative{}{\varepsilon_j}{\bar{\varepsilon}_k}\right|_{\varepsilon_{j}=\varepsilon_{k}=0}\left(\log\left|F^{(\varepsilon\mu)_{jk}}\right|^{2}-2\left(F^{(\varepsilon\mu)_{jk}}\right)^{\ast}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varphi^{(\varepsilon\mu)_{jk}}}\right)\left|\frac{\partial_{w}F^{(\varepsilon\mu)_{jk}}}{F^{(\varepsilon\mu)_{jk}}}\right|\right)(w)\right\}\\
&\hskip-36.98866pt=-2\lim_{w\to\infty}\left\{\frac{1}{|w|}\left.\partialderivative{}{\varepsilon_j}{\bar{\varepsilon}_k}\right|_{\varepsilon_{j}=\varepsilon_{k}=0}\left(F^{(\varepsilon\mu)_{jk}}\right)^{\ast}\left(e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varphi^{(\varepsilon\mu)_{jk}}}\right)\right.\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt+\left.e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varphi(w)}\left.\partialderivative{\varepsilon_j}\right|_{\varepsilon_{j}=0}\left(\frac{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon_{j}\mu_{j}}(w)}{F^{\varepsilon_{j}\mu_{j}}(w)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left.\partialderivative{\bar{\varepsilon}_k}\right|_{\varepsilon_{k}=0}\left(\frac{\overline{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon_{k}\mu_{k}}(w)}}{\overline{F^{\varepsilon_{k}\mu_{k}}(w)}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\\
&\hskip-39.83368pt\overset{\eqref{expphivariations}}{=}\lim_{w\to\infty}\left\{\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varphi(w)}}{2|w|}f_{\mu_{j}\bar{\mu}_{k}}\circ J^{-1}-\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\varphi(w)}}{2}\frac{\left(w\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{j}(w)-\dot{F}^{j}(w)\right)}{|w|\bar{w}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{\left(\overline{w\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{k}(w)-\dot{F}^{k}(w)}\right)}{|w|w^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\}\\
&\hskip-36.98866pt=\lim_{w\to\infty}\left\{\frac{|w|\log|w|}{2|w|}f_{\mu_{j}\bar{\mu}_{k}}\left(J^{-1}(w)\right)\right\}\overset{\eqref{diagJexpansion}}{=}\frac{1}{2}\lim_{w\to\infty}\frac{\log|w|}{\imaginary(J^{-1}(w))}(\imaginary z)f_{\mu_{j}\bar{\mu}_{k}}(z)\\
&\hskip-36.98866pt=\pi\lim_{w\to\infty}(\imaginary z)f_{\mu_{j}\bar{\mu}_{k}}(z)\overset{\eqref{constcuspmetric}}{=}\frac{4\pi}{3}\left\langle\partialderivative{w_j},\partialderivative{w_k}\right\rangle_{\text{TZ},n}^{\text{cusp}}.\end{split}

In the above equation, we have used the fact that
\log|w|/\imaginary(J^{-1}(w))=2\pi
as
w\to\infty
.

∎

Using Lemmas
3.2
and
200
, we can deduce the following corollary:

Corollary 5.1
.

The function
-\log\mathsf{H}=-m_{1}h_{1}\log\mathsf{h}_{1}\dots-m_{n-1}h_{n-1}\log\mathsf{h}_{n-1}+\log\mathsf{h}_{n}
is a Kähler potential for the combination
\frac{4\pi}{3}\omega_{TZ}^{cusp}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}m_{j}h_{j}\hskip 1.42262pt\omega_{\text{TZ},j}^{\text{ell}}
on
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
. The first Chern form of the Hermitian holomorphic
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
(\lambda_{0,\boldsymbol{m}},\mathsf{H})
over
\mathfrak{M}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
is given by

\mathsf{c}_{1}\left(\lambda_{0,\boldsymbol{m}},\mathsf{H}\right)=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\bar{\partial}\partial\log{\mathsf{H}}=\frac{4\pi}{3}\omega_{\text{TZ}}^{\text{cusp}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}m_{j}h_{j}\hskip 1.42262pt\omega_{\text{TZ},j}^{\text{ell}}.

(201)

Remark 5.1
.

In analogy with the famous accessory parameters that are generated by
S_{\boldsymbol{m}}
, the authors of
Can:2017ycp
have defined the so-called “
auxiliary parameters
’’ as
76
76

76

Our definition of auxiliary parameters differs from that of reference
Can:2017ycp
by a factor of
\tfrac{1}{2}
.

d_{i}:=\frac{1}{\mathsf{H}}\partialderivative{\mathsf{H}}{w_i}.

(202)

As emphasized in
Can:2017ycp
, the auxiliary parameters play an equally important role as the accessory parameters. In particular, it follows from the above corollary that an analog of the relation (
160
) can be found between the auxiliary parameters and Takhtajan-Zograf metrics.

Next, Let us remind that the decomposition of
r(z)
is given by

r(z)=\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}\mathrm{a}_{i}\,r_{i}(z),

(203)

where

\mathrm{a}_{i}=\iint_{\mathcal{F}(\Gamma)}\text{Sch}\left(J;z\right)\mu_{i}(z)\differential[2]{z}.

(204)

According to Section
3.2
, for varying
\Gamma
, the
r(z)
determines a
(1,0)
-form
\mathrm{r}
on
\mathcal{T}_{0,n}
and the latter one is corresponding to the
(1,0)
-form
\vartheta
,
77
77

77

See Lemma
3.3
.

\vartheta=\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}\mathrm{a}_{i}\differential{w_i},

on
\mathcal{M}_{0,n}
. Therefore, we also have:

Corollary 5.2
.

The function
\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}:\mathcal{M}_{0,n}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfies

\partial\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}=2\vartheta

(205)

and

\bar{\partial}\partial\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}=-2\sqrt{-1}\left[\omega_{\text{WP}}-\frac{4\pi^{2}}{3}\omega_{\text{TZ}}^{\text{cusp}}-\frac{\pi}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}m_{j}h_{j}\hskip 1.42262pt\omega^{\text{ell}}_{\text{TZ},j}\right].

(206)

To see the (
205
), put
\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}=S_{\boldsymbol{m}}-\pi\log\mathsf{H}
and combine Lemma
5.1
with the proof of Theorem
142
which it gives

\begin{split}\partial\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}&=\partial S_{\boldsymbol{m}}-\pi\sum_{j=1}^{n}m_{j}h_{j}\hskip 1.42262pt\partial\log\mathsf{h}_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}\left(\frac{\partial S_{\boldsymbol{m}}}{\partial{w_{i}}}\right)dw_{i}-\pi\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}m_{j}h_{j}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial w_{i}}\log\mathsf{h}_{j}\right)dw_{i}\\
&=-2\pi\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}c_{i}dw_{i}-\pi\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{j}\dot{F}^{i}_{w}(w_{j})dw_{i}=2\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}\left(-\pi c_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{j}(\mathscr{E}_{j},M_{i})\right)dw_{i}\\
&\overset{\ref {EnergyMomentumEi}}{=}2\sum_{i=1}^{n-3}\mathrm{a}_{i}dw_{i}=2\vartheta\end{split}

In the second line of the above equation, we noted that the Eq.(
90
) implies that

\dot{F}^{i}_{w}(w)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}M_{i}(w^{\prime})\hskip 1.42262pt\partial_{w}R(w^{\prime},w)d^{2}w^{\prime}=-\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}M_{i}(w^{\prime})\left(\frac{1}{(w^{\prime}-w)^{2}}-\frac{1}{w^{\prime}(w^{\prime}-1)}\right)d^{2}w^{\prime},

which at the point
w=w_{j}
is simplified to

\begin{split}\dot{F}^{i}_{w}(w_{j})&=-\frac{1}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}M_{i}(w)\left(\frac{1}{(w-w_{j})^{2}}-\frac{1}{w_{j}(w_{j}-1)}\right)d^{2}w=-\frac{2}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}M_{i}(w)\mathscr{E}_{j}(w)d^{2}w\\
&=-\frac{2}{\pi}\iint_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathscr{E}_{j},M_{i}).\end{split}

The Eq.(
206
) can be obtained easily by using Theorem
159
as well as Lemma
200
.

Remark 5.2
.

According to the Lemmas
3.2
and
4.1
, the functions
\mathsf{H}
and
\exp[S_{\boldsymbol{m}}/\pi]
are Hermitian metrics in the holomorphic
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
\lambda_{0,\boldsymbol{m}}
over
\mathfrak{M}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
. This implies that
\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}=S_{\boldsymbol{m}}-\pi\log\mathsf{H}
determines a function on
\mathfrak{M}_{0,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
. Interestingly, the combination
\omega_{{}_{\text{WP}}}-\tfrac{4\pi^{2}}{3}\omega_{{}_{\text{TZ}}}^{\text{cusp}}-\tfrac{\pi}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}m_{j}h_{j}\hskip 1.42262pt\omega^{\text{ell}}_{\text{TZ},j}
in Eq.(
206
) with overall factor of
\tfrac{1}{12\pi}
appears also in the local index theorem for orbifold Riemann surfaces for
k=0,1
. This is the sign that the function
\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}
can play the role of the
Quillen’s metric
in the Hodge line bundle
\lambda_{1}
, defined in
ZT_2018
. We will explain a little bit more about this observation in conclusion.

5.2
Chern Forms and Potentials on Schottky Space
\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})

Let
O\cong\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\Omega}/\penalty\Sigma=[\mathbb{H}/\penalty\Gamma]
and
\Gamma
be respectively an orbifold Riemann surface and Fuchsian group of signature
(g;m_{1},\dots,m_{n_{e}};n_{p})
and let
J:\mathbb{H}\to\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\Omega}
be the corresponding orbifold covering map. As we have explained in Section
3.3
, the automorphic form
\text{Sch}\left(J^{-1};w\right)
of weight four for the Schottky group can be projected to the subspace
\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\Omega},\Sigma)\cong T_{\pi\circ\Phi(0)}^{\ast}\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
,

\mathsf{R}(w)=\sum_{i=1}^{3g-3+n}\mathrm{b}_{i}P_{i}(w)=\sum_{i=1}^{3g-3+n}\left(\text{Sch}\left(J^{-1}\right),M_{i}\right)P_{i}(w),

(207)

where
P_{i},i=1,\dots,3g-3+n
are given by (
123
) and (
125
) and by using Eq.(
127
) we get

\mathsf{R}(w)=\sum_{i=1}^{3g-3+n}\left(-\pi c_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{j}\left(\mathscr{E}_{j},M_{i}\right)\right)P_{i}(w)\equiv\pi\mathsf{R}_{0}(w)+\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{j}\mathsf{R}_{j}(w).

(208)

The definition of
\mathscr{E}_{j}
is provided by (
128
). The
\mathsf{R}(w)
coincides with a
(1,0)
-form
\mathscr{Q}
on the Schottky pace
\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})

\begin{split}\mathscr{Q}=\sum_{i=1}^{3g-3+n}\mathrm{b}_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}dw_{i}}&=\mathrm{b}_{1}d\lambda_{1}+\dots+\mathrm{b}_{g}d\lambda_{g}+\mathrm{b}_{g+1}da_{3}+\dots+\mathrm{b}_{2g-3}da_{g}\\
&+\mathrm{b}_{2g-2}db_{2}+\dots+\mathrm{b}_{3g-3}db_{g}+\mathrm{b}_{3g-2}dw_{1}+\dots+\mathrm{b}_{3g-3+n}dw_{n}.\end{split}

(209)

In the following two theorems, which can be regarded as generalizations of Theorems 1 and 2 of
park2015potentials
, we will explicitly describe canonical connections and curvature forms of the Hermitian holomorphic (
\mathbb{Q}
-)line bundles
\mathscr{L}_{i}
and
\mathscr{L}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\mathscr{L}_{i}^{\mathsf{h}_{i}}
:

Theorem 1
.

Let
\partial
and
\bar{\partial}
be
(1,0)
and
(0,1)
components of the de Rham differential on Schottky space
\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})
. The following statements are true.

(i)

On the Hermitian holomorphic line bundle
(\mathscr{L}_{i},\mathsf{h}_{i}^{m_{i}})
, the canonical connection is given by
78
78

78

When
m_{i}=\infty
, we will simply ignore
m_{i}
in the following formula.

\partial\log\mathsf{h}_{i}^{m_{i}}=-\frac{2}{\pi}\mathsf{R}_{i}.

(ii)

On the Hermitian holomorphic
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle
(\mathscr{L},e^{\frac{S_{\boldsymbol{m}}}{\pi}})
, the canonical connection is given by

\frac{1}{\pi}\partial S_{\boldsymbol{m}}=2\mathsf{R}_{0}.

(iii)

The function
\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}:\mathfrak{S}_{g,n}(\boldsymbol{m})\to\mathbb{R}
given by Eq.(
197
) satisfies

\partial\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{m}}=2\mathscr{Q}.

Proof.

We will prove each statement separately:

•

In order to prove part
(i)
, it is sufficient to show that

\left.\left(\partialderivative{\log\Lponetial_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_j}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\left\{\begin{split}&-\frac{2}{\pi m_{i}}\left(\mathscr{E}_{i},M_{j}\right)\quad&\text{for}\quad&i=1,\dots,n_{e},\\
&-\frac{2}{\pi}\left(\mathscr{E}_{i},M_{j}\right)&\text{for}\quad&i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n.\end{split}\right.

Using Lemma
5.1
, we have

\left.\left(\partialderivative{\log\Lponetial_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_j}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right|_{\varepsilon=0}=\left\{\begin{split}&\frac{1}{m_{i}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{j}(w_{i})\quad&\text{for}\quad&i=1,\dots,n_{e},\\
&\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{j}(w_{i})&\text{for}\quad&i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n.\end{split}\right.

(210)

In another side, according to (
90
) and (
128
), one see

\pi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{j}(w_{i})=-\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}}M_{j}(w)\left(\frac{1}{(w-w_{i})^{2}}-\frac{1}{w(w-1)}\right)\differential[2]{w}=-2\left(\mathscr{E}_{i},M_{j}\right),

which by substituting in (
210
) gives the desired result.

•

To prove part
(ii)
, we need to show that

\left.\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}S_{\boldsymbol{m}}([L_{1}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}},\dots,L_{g}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}];w_{1}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}},\dots,w_{n}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})=-2\pi c_{i}\quad\text{for}\quad i=1,\dots,3g-3+n.

We have

\begin{split}&\mathcal{L}_{\mu_{i}}S_{\boldsymbol{m}}=\left.\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}S_{\boldsymbol{m}}\left([L_{1}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}},\dots,L_{g}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}];w_{1}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}},\dots,w_{n}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\right)\\
&\hskip 31.2982pt=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\left.\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}I_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)+\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\frac{\partial\tilde{S}^{\text{(ct)}\epsilon}}{\partial w_{i}},\end{split}

(211)

with
\tilde{S}^{\text{(ct)}\epsilon}
is given in equation (
144
) and

\begin{split}&\hskip-28.45274ptI_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)=\iint_{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon})}|\partial_{w}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}+\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(C_{k})}\theta_{(L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{-1}}(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(C_{j}^{\epsilon})}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\overline{w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\right),\end{split}

where once again, we have used the Gauss-Bonnet formula for Riemann orbisurfaces
Troyanov1991PrescribingCO
;
Li_orbifold_2018

\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\iint_{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}})}e^{\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}=2\pi\left(2g+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)+n_{p}-2\right)=-2\pi\chi(X),

to conclude that

\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}\mathcal{L}_{\mu_{i}}\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}}e^{\varphi}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}=0.

The calculation of
\mathcal{L}_{\mu_{i}}S_{\boldsymbol{m}}
closely follows the corresponding computation in the proof of Theorem 1 in
1988SbMat..60..297Z
, where regularization at the punctures can be found in the proof of Theorem 1 in
Zograf1988ONLE
and for branch points in the proof of Theorem
142
. More explicitly, by applying the change of variable formula
\int_{F(\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}})}\omega=\int_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}}F^{\ast}(\omega)
and noting to the commutative diagram
129
, one finds

\begin{split}I_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)&=\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}(F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\ast}\Big(|\partial_{w}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\Big)+\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}(F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\ast}\left(\theta_{(L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{-1}}(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})\right)\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}(F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\ast}\left(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\left(\frac{\differential{\bar{w}}}{\bar{w}-\overline{w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}-\frac{\differential{w}}{w-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\right)\right)\\
\\
&=\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}|\partial_{w}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}\differential{F^{\varepsilon\mu_i}(w)}\wedge\differential{\overline{F^{\varepsilon\mu_i}(w)}}\\
&+\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\left(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}-\frac{1}{2}\log|(L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\prime}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}-\log|l_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}\right)\\
&\hskip 79.6678pt\times\left(\frac{(L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\prime\prime}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}{(L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\prime}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\differential{F^{\varepsilon\mu_i}(w)}-\frac{\overline{(L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\prime\prime}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}{\overline{(L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\prime}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}\differential{\overline{F^{\varepsilon\mu_i}(w)}}\right)\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})\left(\frac{\differential{\overline{F^{\varepsilon\mu_i}(w)}}}{\overline{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(w)}-\overline{w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}-\frac{\differential{F^{\varepsilon\mu_i}(w)}}{F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(w)-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\right).\end{split}

which by noting that
\differential{F^{\varepsilon\mu_i}(w)}=\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(dw+\varepsilon M_{i}\hskip 1.42262ptd\bar{w})
and
\differential{\overline{F^{\varepsilon\mu_i}(w)}}=\overline{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}(\overline{\varepsilon}\overline{M_{i}}dw+d\bar{w})
, it turns to

\begin{split}I_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)&=\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}|\partial_{w}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}\,|\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}(1-|\varepsilon M_{i}|^{2})\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&\hskip-19.91684pt-2\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\,\frac{\overline{(L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\prime\prime}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}{\overline{(L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\prime}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}\,\overline{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}(\bar{\varepsilon}\overline{M_{i}}\differential{w}+\differential{\bar{w}})\\

&\hskip-19.91684pt-\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\,\log|(L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\prime}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}\,\overline{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}(\bar{\varepsilon}\overline{M_{i}}\differential{w}+\differential{\bar{w}})\\
&\hskip-19.91684pt+\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\log|(L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\prime}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}\,\frac{\overline{(L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\prime\prime}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}{\overline{(L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})^{\prime}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}\,\overline{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}(\bar{\varepsilon}\overline{M_{i}}\differential{w}+\differential{\bar{w}})\\
&\hskip-19.91684pt+8\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{k=2}^{g}\log|l_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|^{2}\\
&\hskip-19.91684pt+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\hskip-2.84526pt\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\hskip-2.84526pt\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}\hskip-2.84526pt(\varphi^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}})\hskip-2.84526pt\left(\frac{\overline{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}(\bar{\varepsilon}\overline{M_{i}}\differential{w}+\differential{\bar{w}})}{\overline{w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}-\overline{w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}}-\frac{\partial_{w}F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}(\differential{w}+\varepsilon M_{i}\differential{\bar{w}})}{w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}}\right)\end{split}

where
C_{k}
s are the usual components of
\partial\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}
and

\left\{\begin{split}&\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)=\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}\Big\backslash\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}D_{i}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon),\\
&D_{i}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)=\left\{w\in\mathcal{D}\,\Big|\,|w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}-w_{i}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|<\epsilon\right\},\\
&C_{j}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)=\partial D_{j}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)=\left\{w\in\mathcal{D}\,\Big|\,|w^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}-w_{j}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}|=\epsilon\right\}.\end{split}\right.

Just as in the proof of Theorem
142
, to calculate
\dot{I}^{i}_{\epsilon}
, we must differentiate both the integrand and the integration domain
\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)
.
Using Eq.(
151
), the second contribution gives

\left.\partialderivative{\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}=-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\oint_{\partial D_{j}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\left(\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})\right)\differential{\bar{w}},

(212)

where the boundaries
\partial D_{j}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)
are oriented in a manner that is counter to the orientation of
\partial\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}
. These boundaries also oriented as a boundary of
D_{j}^{\epsilon}(\varepsilon)
. Moreover, the contribution from the variation of integral domain

vanishes since

. The differentiation under the integral sign repeats the calculations done in the proof of
(
1988SbMat..60..297Z
, Theorem 1)
almost word-for-word. The only change is that the integration domain has now changed to
\mathcal{D}_{\epsilon}
. Accordingly,

\begin{split}\dot{I}^{i}_{\epsilon}&=\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}\left[(\partial_{w}\dot{\varphi}^{i}+\partial_{w}^{2}\varphi\dot{F}^{i})\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi+(\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{\varphi}^{i}+\partial_{w}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\dot{F}^{i})\partial_{w}\varphi+|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\right]\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\oint_{\partial D_{j}^{\epsilon}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\left(\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})\right)\differential{\bar{w}}-2\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}(\dot{\varphi}^{i}+\partial_{w}\varphi\dot{F}^{i})\frac{\overline{L_{k}^{\prime\prime}}}{\overline{L_{k}^{\prime}}}\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\left[(\partial_{w}\dot{\varphi}^{i}+\partial_{w}^{2}\varphi\dot{F}^{i})\log|L_{k}^{\prime}|^{2}\differential{w}+\partial_{w}\varphi\frac{(\dot{L}_{k}^{i})^{\prime}+L_{k}^{\prime\prime}\dot{F}^{i}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\differential{w}\right.\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt+\partial_{w}\varphi\log|L^{\prime}_{k}|^{2}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w}+\partial_{w}\varphi\log|L^{\prime}_{k}|^{2}M_{i}\differential{\bar{w}}\Bigg]\\
&-\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\left[(\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{\varphi}^{i}+\partial_{w}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\dot{F}^{i})\log|L_{k}^{\prime}|^{2}+\partial_{w}\varphi\frac{(\dot{L}_{k}^{i})^{\prime}+L_{k}^{\prime\prime}\dot{F}^{i}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\right]\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&+\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\frac{(\dot{L}_{k}^{i})^{\prime}+L_{k}^{\prime\prime}\dot{F}^{i}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\frac{\overline{L_{k}^{\prime\prime}}}{\overline{L_{k}^{\prime}}}\differential{\bar{w}}+8\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{k=2}^{g}\frac{\dot{l}_{k}^{i}}{l_{k}}\\
&-\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\left(\frac{d\bar{w}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{dw}{w-w_{j}}\right)+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right),\end{split}

where we have used Eq.(
153
) and the fact that
F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}
and
L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}
are holomorphic in the variable
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{C}
. As in the proof of Theorem

, we can use Lemma
3.4
and the equality

to rewrite the above equation as

\begin{split}\dot{I}^{i}_{\epsilon}&=\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}\left[(-\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}-\partial_{w}^{2}\dot{F}^{i})\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi+(-\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}-\partial_{w}\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i})\partial_{w}\varphi+|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\right]\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\oint_{\partial D_{j}^{\epsilon}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\left(\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})\right)\differential{\bar{w}}+2\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\frac{\overline{L_{k}^{\prime\prime}}}{\overline{L_{k}^{\prime}}}\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\left[(-\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}-\partial_{w}^{2}\dot{F}^{i})\log|L_{k}^{\prime}|^{2}\differential{w}+\partial_{w}\varphi\frac{(\dot{L}_{k}^{i})^{\prime}+L_{k}^{\prime\prime}\dot{F}^{i}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\differential{w}\right.\\
&\hskip 105.2751pt+\partial_{w}\varphi\log|L^{\prime}_{k}|^{2}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w}+\partial_{w}\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}\log|L^{\prime}_{k}|^{2}\differential{\bar{w}}\Bigg]\\
&-\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\left(\partial_{w}\varphi-\frac{\overline{L_{k}^{\prime\prime}}}{\overline{L_{k}^{\prime}}}\right)\frac{(\dot{L}_{k}^{i})^{\prime}+L_{k}^{\prime\prime}\dot{F}^{i}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\differential{\bar{w}}+8\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{k=2}^{g}\frac{\dot{l}_{k}^{i}}{l_{k}}\\
&-\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\left(\frac{d\bar{w}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{dw}{w-w_{j}}\right)+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right).\end{split}

(213)

Let us first compute the integral over
\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}
:

\begin{split}&\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}\left[(-\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}-\partial_{w}^{2}\dot{F}^{i})\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi+(-\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}-\partial_{w}\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i})\partial_{w}\varphi+|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\right]\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&=\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}\left[\big(2\partial_{w}^{2}\varphi-(\partial_{w}\varphi)^{2}\big)\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}-2\partialderivative{w}(\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^i)\right.\\
&\hskip 123.76965pt\left.+\partialderivative{\bar{w}}(\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^i)-\partialderivative{w}(\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^i)\right]\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&=2\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}T_{\varphi}M_{i}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\underbrace{-2\int_{\partial\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}}_{I_{1}}\underbrace{-\int_{\partial\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w}}_{I_{2}}\underbrace{-\int_{\partial\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}}_{I_{3}},\end{split}

where in the last line, we have used the definition of the Energy-Momentum
T_{\varphi}=\text{Sch}\left(J^{-1};w\right)=\partial_{w}^{2}\varphi-\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{w}\varphi)^{2}
. In order to compute the integrals
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
, and
I_{3}
, we need some useful identities. From the equality

F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ L_{k}=L_{k}^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}\circ F^{\varepsilon\mu_{i}}
for
k=1,\dots,g

one can see that

\left\{\begin{split}&\dot{F}^{i}\circ L_{k}=\dot{F}^{i}L^{\prime}_{k}+\dot{L}_{k}^{i},\\
\\
&\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\circ L_{k}\,L^{\prime}_{k}=\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}L^{\prime}_{k}+\dot{F}^{i}L^{\prime\prime}_{k}+\dot{L}_{k}^{i},\\
\\
&\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}\circ L_{k}\,\overline{L^{\prime}_{k}}=\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}L^{\prime}_{k},\end{split}\right.

Moreover, from Eq.(
132
) one has

\partial_{w}\varphi\circ L_{k}\,L^{\prime}_{k}+\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}=\partial_{w}\varphi,\qquad\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\circ L_{k}\,\overline{L^{\prime}_{k}}+\frac{\overline{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}}{\overline{L^{\prime}_{k}}}=\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi.

Now, recalling that
\partial\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}=\left[\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{g}(C_{k}\cup C^{\prime}_{k})\right]\cup\left[\bigcup\limits_{i=1}^{n+l}C_{i}^{\epsilon}\right]
where
C^{\prime}_{k}=-L_{k}(C_{k})
, together with implementing some of the above identities we get

\begin{split}I_{1}&=-2\int_{\partial\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&=-2\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}-2\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{j}}\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}-2\sum_{j=1}^{n_{p}}\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{n_{e}+j}}\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&=-2\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\dot{F}^{i}\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\differential{\bar{w}}-2\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{j}}\partial_{w}\varphi M_{i}\differential{\bar{w}}-2\sum_{j=1}^{n_{p}}\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{n_{e}+j}}\partial_{w}\varphi M_{i}\differential{\bar{w}},\end{split}

which it can be simplified more by using the asymptotic expansions (
320
)

\begin{split}&I_{1}=2\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\left[\left(\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\right)^{\prime}\dot{F}^{i}+\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\right]\differential{w}\\
&-2\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{j}}\left(-\frac{1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}}{w-w_{j}}+\frac{c_{j}}{1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}}+\dotsm\right)\left(\frac{\bar{q}^{(j)}_{1}}{4\bar{J}^{(j)}_{1}}\cdot|w-w_{j}|^{1-\frac{2}{m_{j}}}+\dotsm\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-2\sum_{j=1}^{n_{p}-1}\oint_{C^{\epsilon}_{n_{e}+j}}\left(-\frac{1}{w-w_{n_{e}+j}}\left(1+\frac{1}{\log\left|\frac{w-w_{n_{e}+j}}{J_{1}^{(n_{e}+j)}}\right|}\right)+\dotsm\right)\times\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\times\left(-\frac{|\delta_{n_{e}+j}|^{2}\bar{q}_{1}^{(n_{e}+j)}}{4\pi^{2}\bar{J}_{1}^{(n_{e}+j)}}\cdot|w-w_{n_{e}+j}|\log^{2}|w-w_{n_{e}+j}|+\dotsm\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&-2\oint_{C_{n}^{\epsilon}}\left(-\frac{1}{w}\left(1+\frac{1}{\log\left|\frac{w}{J_{-1}^{(n)}}\right|}\right)-\frac{c_{n}}{w^{2}}+\dotsm\right)\left(-\frac{|\delta_{n}|^{2}\bar{q}_{1}^{(n)}\bar{J}_{-1}^{(n)}}{4\pi^{2}}\cdot\frac{\log^{2}|w|}{|w|}+\dotsm\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&=2\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\left[\left(\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\right)^{\prime}\dot{F}^{i}+\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\right]\differential{w}+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right);\end{split}

Moreover,

\begin{split}I_{2}&=-\int_{\partial\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w}=-\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w}\\
&=-\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\left[\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}-\left(\partial_{w}\varphi-\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\right)\frac{(\dot{L}_{k}^{i})^{\prime}+L_{k}^{\prime\prime}\dot{F}^{i}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\right]\differential{w}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w};\end{split}

Furthermore,

\begin{split}I_{3}&=-\int_{\partial\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}=-\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&=-\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\left[\frac{\overline{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}}{\overline{L^{\prime}_{k}}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}-\left(\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi-\frac{\overline{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}}{\overline{L^{\prime}_{k}}}\right)\frac{(\dot{L}_{k}^{i})^{\prime}+L_{k}^{\prime\prime}\dot{F}^{i}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\right]\differential{\bar{w}}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}};\end{split}

Using the fact that

-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}=-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\varphi}=\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)\quad\text{as}\quad\epsilon\to 0,

we get

\begin{split}I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}&=2\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\left[\left(\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\right)^{\prime}\dot{F}^{i}+\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\right]\differential{w}\\
&-\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\left[\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}-\left(\partial_{w}\varphi-\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\right)\frac{(\dot{L}_{k}^{i})^{\prime}+L_{k}^{\prime\prime}\dot{F}^{i}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\right]\differential{w}\\
&-\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\left[\frac{\overline{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}}{\overline{L^{\prime}_{k}}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}-\left(\partial_{\bar{w}}\varphi-\frac{\overline{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}}{\overline{L^{\prime}_{k}}}\right)\frac{(\dot{L}_{k}^{i})^{\prime}+L_{k}^{\prime\prime}\dot{F}^{i}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\right]\differential{\bar{w}}+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right).\end{split}

Substituting the above results in Eq.(
213
), we get

\begin{split}\dot{I}^{i}_{\epsilon}&=2\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}T_{\varphi}M_{i}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\left(\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&+\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\left[\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\left(\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\differential{w}+\frac{\overline{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}}{\overline{L^{\prime}_{k}}}\differential{\bar{w}}\right)+\log|L^{\prime}_{k}|^{2}\left(\partial_{w}^{2}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w}+\partial_{\bar{w}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}\right)\right]\\
&+\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\dot{F}^{i}\left[2\left(\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\right)^{2}\right]\differential{w}\\
&+\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\frac{(\dot{L}_{k}^{i})^{\prime}+L_{k}^{\prime\prime}}{(L^{\prime}_{k})^{2}}\differential{w}+8\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{k=2}^{g}\frac{\dot{l}_{k}^{i}}{l_{k}}\\
&-\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\left(\frac{d\bar{w}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{dw}{w-w_{j}}\right)+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right).\end{split}

(214)

Using the fact that the second line in (
214
) can be written as

\begin{split}&\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\left[\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\left(\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\differential{w}+\frac{\overline{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}}{\overline{L^{\prime}_{k}}}\differential{\bar{w}}\right)+\log|L^{\prime}_{k}|^{2}\left(\partial_{w}^{2}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{w}+\partial_{\bar{w}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\differential{\bar{w}}\right)\right]\\
&=\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\differential{\left(\partial_{w} \dot{F}^i \log|L'_k|^2\right)},\end{split}

one can see that this sum vanishes; the third line in (
214
) is also equal to
0
, since

\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\dot{F}^{i}\left[2\left(\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{L^{\prime\prime}_{k}}{L^{\prime}_{k}}\right)^{2}\right]\differential{w}=2\sum_{k=2}^{g}\oint_{C_{k}}\dot{F}^{i}\hskip 2.84526pt\text{Sch}\left(L_{k};w\right)\differential{w}=0.

As for the fourth line of Eq.(
214
), since the contour
C_{k}
encircles a zero of the function
L^{\prime}_{k}
, i.e. the point
L_{k}^{-1}(\infty)
, we get from the Cauchy formula that

\oint_{C_{k}}\frac{(\dot{L}_{k}^{i})^{\prime}+L_{k}^{\prime\prime}}{(L^{\prime}_{k})^{2}}\differential{w}=-8\pi\sqrt{-1}\frac{\dot{l}_{k}^{i}}{l_{k}}\quad\text{for}\quad k=2,\dots,g;

therefore, the fourth line of Eq.(
214
) vanishes as well. Putting everything together, one has

\begin{split}\dot{I}^{i}_{\epsilon}&=2\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}T_{\varphi}M_{i}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\left(\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}\left(\frac{d\bar{w}}{\bar{w}-\bar{w}_{j}}-\frac{dw}{w-w_{j}}\right)+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right).\end{split}

(215)

Using Eq.(
211
), the limit

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\iint_{\overset{{}_{\curlywedge}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\epsilon}}T_{\varphi}M_{i}\differential{w}\wedge\differential{\bar{w}}\overset{\eqref{Rquaddiffexpansion}}{=}-2\sqrt{-1}\mathrm{b}_{i},

and

\begin{split}&\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}|\partial_{w}\varphi|^{2}\left(\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&\overset{\ref {lemma:asymptotics}}{=}\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)^{2}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\frac{\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})}{|w-w_{j}|^{2}}\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&+\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{p}-1}\oint_{C_{j}^{\epsilon}}\left(\frac{\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})}{|w-w_{j}|^{2}}+\frac{2\big(\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})\big)}{|w-w_{j}|^{2}\log|w-w_{j}|}\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&+\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\oint_{C_{n}^{\epsilon}}\left(\frac{\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(\infty)}{|w|^{2}}+\frac{2\big(\dot{F}^{i}(w)-\dot{F}^{i}(\infty)\big)}{|w|^{2}\log|w|}\right)\differential{\bar{w}}\\
&=2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)^{2}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})\quad\text{with}\quad m_{j}=\infty\quad\text{for}\quad j=n_{e}+1,\dots,n,\end{split}

Accordingly, the Eq.(
215
) is simplified to

\begin{split}\dot{I}^{i}_{\epsilon}=-4\sqrt{-1}\mathrm{b}_{i}-2\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)^{2}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})-4\pi\sqrt{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{e}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{j}}\right)\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})\end{split}

(216)

Finally, by putting everything together from (
211
), (

) and (
158
), we get

\begin{split}&\mathcal{L}_{\mu_{i}}S_{\boldsymbol{m}}=2\mathrm{b}_{i}+\pi\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{j}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})=-2\pi c_{i}+2\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{j}\left(\mathscr{E}_{j},M_{i}\right)+\pi\sum_{j=1}^{n}h_{j}\partial_{w}\dot{F}^{i}(w_{j})\\
&\overset{\eqref{EMinnerprod}}{=}-2\pi c_{i}.\end{split}

•

The proof of part

follows readily from those of parts

and
(ii)
.
