near these parabolic and elliptic fixed-points:

•

Asymptotic form of
\varphi(w)
near cusps
:

\varphi(w)=\left\{\begin{split}&-2\log|w-w_{i}|-2\log\left|\log|w-w_{i}|\right|+\order{1}\quad(w_{i}\neq\infty),\qquad&w\to w_{i},\\
&-2\log|w|-2\log\log|w|+\order{1},&w\to\infty;\end{split}\right.

(292)

•

Asymptotic form of
\varphi(w)
near cones
:

\varphi(w)=\left\{\begin{split}&-2(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}})\log|w-w_{i}|+\order{1}\quad(w_{i}\neq\infty),\qquad&w\to w_{i},\\
\end{split}\right.

(293)

One can also derive these asymptotics by studying the mapping
J
, called
Klein’s Hauptmodule
, as a meromorphic function on
\mathbb{H}
which is automorphic with respect to the Fuchsian group
\Gamma
– i.e.

J(\gamma z)=J(z)\hskip 28.45274pt\text{for}\hskip 5.69046ptz\in\mathbb{H}\hskip 5.69046pt\text{and}\hskip 5.69046pt\forall\gamma\in\Gamma

(294)

For the sake of simplicity, let us assume that the Fuchsian group
\Gamma
has genus
0
; we can choose a standard system of
n_{e}
elliptic generators
\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n_{e}}
of orders
m_{1},\dots,m_{n_{e}}
and
n_{p}
parabolic generators
\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{n_{p}}
(
n_{e}+n_{p}=n
) satisfying the single relation
\tau_{1}\dotsm\tau_{n_{e}}\kappa_{1}\dotsm\kappa_{n_{p}}=1
. Let
z_{1},\dots,z_{n_{e}}\in\hat{\mathbb{C}}
and
z_{n_{e}+1},\dots,z_{n}\in\mathbb{R}\cup\{\infty\}
be the fixed points of elliptic and parabolic generators respectively which project into
w_{1},\dots,w_{n_{e}},w_{n_{e}+1},\dots,w_{n}\in\hat{\mathbb{C}}
. We will assume that
z_{n-2}=0,z_{n-1}=1,
and
z_{n}=\infty
, which can always be achieved by conjugation with
\operatorname{PSL}(2,\mathbb{R})
. The elements
\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n_{e}}
and
\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{n_{p}}
are then represented in their
matrix form
as

\tau_{i}=\matrixquantity(\lx@physics@matrix z_{i} - \lambda_i \bar{z}_{i} & (\lambda_i -1) z_{i} \bar{z}_{i} \\ 1-\lambda_i & \lambda_i z_{i} - \bar{z}_{i}\endlx@physics@matrix),\quad\kappa_{j}=\matrixquantity(\lx@physics@matrix 1+\delta_{n_e+j}\hspace{.3mm} z_{n_e+j} & - \delta_{n_e+j}\hspace{.3mm} z_{n_e+j}^2 \\ \delta_{n_e+j} & 1-\delta_{n_e+j}\hspace{.3mm} z_{n_e+j}\endlx@physics@matrix),\quad\kappa_{n_{p}}=\matrixquantity(\lx@physics@matrix 1 & \delta_n \\ 0 & 1\endlx@physics@matrix),

(295)

with
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
and
j=1,\dots,n_{p}-1
. In the above equation,
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{n_{e}}=e^{\frac{2\pi i}{m_{1}}},\dots,e^{\frac{2\pi i}{m_{n_{e}}}}
are called the
multipliers
of
\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n_{e}}
and
\delta_{n_{e}+1},\dots,\delta_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
are called
translation lengths
of
\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{n_{p}}
. Therefore, according to the Eq.(
294
) and using (
295
), it is easy to see
that in a neighborhood of each elliptic point
z_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
with ramification index
m_{i}
, the Hauptmodule can be expanded as

J(z)=w_{i}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}J^{(i)}_{k}\left(\frac{z-z_{i}}{z-\bar{z}_{i}}\right)^{km_{i}},

(296)

and in a neighborhood of each of the parabolic points
z_{i}
,
i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
:

J(z)=w_{i}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}J^{(i)}_{k}\exp(-\frac{2 \pi\sqrt{-1}k}{|\delta_{i}|(z-z_{i})}).

(297)

Similarly, in a neighborhood of the parabolic point
z_{n}=\infty
, the function
J(z)
can be expanded as

J(z)=\sum_{k=-1}^{\infty}J^{(n)}_{k}\exp(\frac{2 \pi\sqrt{-1}k z}{|\delta_n|}).

(298)

In equations (
296
), (
297
) and (
298
), the coefficients
J^{(i)}_{1},J^{(n)}_{-1}\neq 0
because the mapping
J
is univalent in any fundamental domain for
\Gamma
. If we choose elements
\varsigma_{n_{e}+j},\dots,\varsigma_{n}\in\operatorname{PSL}(2,\mathbb{R})
such that
\varsigma_{n_{e}+j}(\infty)=z_{n_{e}+j}
and

\varsigma_{n_{e}+j}^{-1}\hskip 2.84526pt\kappa_{j}\hskip 2.84526pt\varsigma_{n_{e}+j}=\matrixquantity(\lx@physics@matrix 1 & \pm 1 \\ 0 & 1\endlx@physics@matrix),

for
j=1,\dots,n_{p}
, we can also rewrite (
297
) and (
298
) in the form

J(\varsigma_{i}z)=\left\{\begin{split}&w_{i}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}J^{(i)}_{k}\exp(2 \pi\sqrt{-1}k z),&\hskip 14.22636pti=n_{e}+1\dots n-1,\\
&\sum_{k=-1}^{\infty}J^{(i)}_{k}\exp(2 \pi\sqrt{-1} k z),\qquad&i=n.\end{split}\right.

(299)

The hyperbolic metric
e^{\varphi(w)}|\differential{w}|^{2}
on
O
is given by

e^{\varphi(w)}=\frac{\left|J^{-1}(w)^{\prime}\right|^{2}}{\left(\imaginary J^{-1}(w)\right)^{2}},

(300)

and satisfies the Liouville’s equation (
78
). In order to find the asymptotic behavior of the Liouville field
\varphi(w)
near branch points and cusps, we need to study the
multivalued
analytic function
J^{-1}:O\to\mathbb{H}
which is a locally univalent linearly polymorphic function
119
119

119

Linearly polymorphic means that the branches of this function are connected by linear fractional transformations in
\Gamma
.
on
O
. Let us first calculate the expansion of
J^{-1}(w)
near elliptic fixed points. To do so, we will rewrite the expansion (
296
) as

J=w_{i}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}J^{(i)}_{k}\left(\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}}^{m_{i}}\right)^{k},

(301)

where
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
and
\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}}\equiv(z-z_{i})/(z-\bar{z}_{i})
is the coordinate on
\mathbb{D}
instead of
\mathbb{H}
. Formally, by inverting the above power series, we get

\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}}=\tilde{J}^{-1}(w)=\left(\frac{1}{J^{(i)}_{1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{i}}}(w-w_{i})^{\frac{1}{m_{i}}}-\frac{J^{(i)}_{2}}{m_{i}\left(J^{(i)}_{1}\right)^{2+\frac{1}{m_{i}}}}(w-w_{i})^{1+\frac{1}{m_{i}}}\\
+\frac{-2m_{i}J^{(i)}_{1}J^{(i)}_{3}+\left(J^{(i)}_{2}\right)^{2}(1+3m_{i})}{2m_{i}^{2}\left(J^{(i)}_{1}\right)^{4+\frac{1}{m_{i}}}}(w-w_{i})^{2+\frac{1}{m_{i}}}+\cdots

(302)

Then, by using the definitions
\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}}
, we have

J^{-1}(w)=z_{i}+2\sqrt{-1}\,\imaginary z_{i}\left(\frac{w-w_{i}}{J^{(i)}_{1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{i}}}\left(1+\frac{J^{(i)}_{2}}{m_{i}\left(J^{(i)}_{1}\right)^{2}}(w-w_{i})+\cdots\right).

(303)

We can use the same method to find the expansion of
J^{-1}
near cusps as well. Near parabolic points by rewriting the equations (
297
) and (
298
) as a power series in
\iota_{j\neq n}\equiv\exp(-\frac{2 \pi\sqrt{-1}}{|\delta_{j}|(z-z_{j})})
and
\iota_{n}\equiv\exp(\frac{2 \pi\sqrt{-1} z}{|\delta_n|})
, we get

J(z)=\left\{\begin{split}&w_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}J^{(j)}_{k}\iota_{j}^{k},&\hskip 14.22636ptj=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1,\\
&\sum_{k=-1}^{\infty}J^{(j)}_{k}\iota_{j}^{k},\qquad&j=n.\end{split}\right.

As before, we can formally invert the above power series (
j=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
)

\left\{\begin{split}&\iota_{j}=\frac{1}{J_{1}^{(j)}}(w-w_{j})-\frac{J_{2}^{(j)}}{\left(J_{1}^{(j)}\right)^{3}}(w-w_{j})^{2}\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{2\left(J_{2}^{(j)}\right)^{2}-J_{1}^{(j)}J_{3}^{(j)}}{\left(J_{1}^{(j)}\right)^{5}}(w-w_{j})^{3}+\cdots,\hskip 76.82234ptw\to w_{j},\\
&\iota_{n}=\frac{J_{-1}^{(n)}}{w}+\frac{J_{-1}^{(n)}J_{0}^{(n)}}{w^{2}}+\frac{J_{-1}^{(n)}\left(\left(J_{0}^{(n)}\right)^{2}-J_{-1}^{(n)}J_{1}^{(n)}\right)}{w^{3}}+\cdots,\hskip 34.14322ptw\to\infty\end{split}\right.

and again using the definitions
\iota_{j\neq n}
and
\iota_{n}
, to see (
j=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
)

J^{-1}(w)=\left\{\begin{split}&z_{j}-\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{|\delta_{j}|}\bigg(\log(\frac{w-w_{j}}{J_1^{(j)}})-\frac{J_{2}^{(j)}}{\left(J_{1}^{(j)}\right)^{2}}(w-w_{j})\hskip 2.84526pt+\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{\frac{3}{2}\left(J_{2}^{(j)}\right)^{2}-J_{1}^{(j)}J_{3}^{(j)}}{\left(J_{1}^{(j)}\right)^{4}}(w-w_{j})^{2}+\cdots\bigg)^{-1},\hskip 62.59596ptw\to w_{j},\\
&\frac{|\delta_{n}|}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}\left(\log(\frac{J_{-1}^{(n)}}{w})+\frac{J_{0}^{(n)}}{w}+\frac{\frac{1}{2}\left(J_{0}^{(n)}\right)^{2}-J_{-1}^{(n)}J_{1}^{(n)}}{w^{2}}+\cdots\right),\hskip 5.69046ptw\to\infty.\end{split}\right.

Let us identify the accessory parameters as (see
lemma 1
of
Zograf1988ONLE
and
lemma 3
of
Takhtajan:2001uj
)

\left\{\begin{split}&c_{i}\equiv-\frac{h_{i}J^{(i)}_{2}}{\left(J^{(i)}_{1}\right)^{2}},\hskip 62.59596pti=1,\dots,n_{e},\\
&c_{i}\equiv-\frac{J^{(i)}_{2}}{\left(J^{(i)}_{1}\right)^{2}},\hskip 59.75095pti=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1,\\
&c_{n}\equiv J_{0}^{(i)},\hskip 88.2037pti=n,\end{split}\right.

where
h_{i}/2\equiv(1-1/m_{i}^{2})/2
is the
conformal weight
of the
twist operators
corresponding to branch points
Knizhnik:1987xp
. Accordingly, we can summarize the asymptotic behavior of the
J^{-1}(w)
near conical singularities and cusps (
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
,
j=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
)

J^{-1}(w)=\left\{\begin{split}&z_{i}+2\sqrt{-1}\,\imaginary z_{i}\left(\frac{w-w_{i}}{J^{(i)}_{1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{i}}}\left(1-\frac{c_{i}}{m_{i}h_{i}}(w-w_{i})+\cdots\right),\hskip 34.14322ptw\to w_{i},\\
&z_{j}-\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{|\delta_{j}|}\left(\log(\frac{w-w_{j}}{J_1^{(j)}})-c_{j}(w-w_{j})+\cdots\right)^{-1}\hskip-14.22636pt,\hskip 71.13188ptw\to w_{j},\\
&\frac{|\delta_{n}|}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}\left(\log(\frac{J_{-1}^{(n)}}{w})+\frac{c_{n}}{w}+\cdots\right),\hskip 147.95424ptw\to\infty.\end{split}\right.

(304)

Finally, we are ready to derive the asymptotic behavior of the Liouville filed
\varphi(w)
and its derivatives using Eq.(
300
) and the above expansion near the cones and cusps:

Lemma C.1
.

The function
\varphi(w)
has the following properties:

1.

\partial_{w}^{2}\varphi-\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{w}\varphi)^{2}=\text{Sch}\left(J^{-1};w\right)
;

2.

For
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
and
j=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
,

\varphi(w)=\left\{\begin{split}&-2(1-\frac{1}{m_{i}})\log|w-w_{i}|+\log\frac{4|J^{(i)}_{1}|^{-\frac{2}{m_{i}}}}{m_{i}^{2}}+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)\hskip 39.83368ptw\to w_{i}\\
&-2\log|w-w_{j}|-2\log\left|\log\left|\frac{w-w_{j}}{J^{(j)}_{1}}\right|\right|+\text{\scriptsize{$\mathcal{O}$}}\left(1\right)\hskip 54.06006ptw\to w_{j},\\
&-2\log|w|-2\log\log\left|\frac{w}{J^{(n)}_{-1}}\right|+\order{|w|^{-1}},\hskip 79.6678ptw\to\infty\end{split}\right.

3.

For
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
and
j=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
,

\left\{\begin{split}&\frac{4|J^{(i)}_{1}|^{-\frac{2}{m_{i}}}}{m_{i}^{2}}=\lim_{w\to w_{i}}e^{\varphi(w)}|w-w_{i}|^{2-\frac{2}{m_{i}}},\\
&\left|J^{(j)}_{1}\right|^{2}=\lim_{w\to w_{j}}\exp(\log|w-w_{j}|^2 - \frac{2 e^{-\frac{\varphi(w)}{2}}}{|w-w_{j}|}),\\
&\left|J^{(n)}_{-1}\right|^{2}=\lim_{w\to\infty}\exp(\log|w|^2 - \frac{2 e^{-\frac{\varphi(w)}{2}}}{|w|}),\end{split}\right.

4.

For
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
and
j=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
,

\partial_{w}\varphi(w)=\left\{\begin{split}&-\frac{1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}}{w-w_{i}}+\frac{c_{i}}{1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}}+\order{1}\hskip 136.5733ptw\to w_{i},\\
&-\frac{1}{w-w_{j}}\left(1+\left(\log\left|\frac{w-w_{j}}{J_{1}^{(j)}}\right|\right)^{-1}\right)+c_{j}+\order{1}\hskip 34.14322ptw\to w_{j},\\
&-\frac{1}{w}\left(1+\left(\log\left|\frac{w}{J_{-1}^{(n)}}\right|\right)^{-1}\right)-\frac{c_{n}}{w^{2}}+\order{\frac{1}{|w|^2}},\hskip 42.67912ptw\to\infty\end{split}\right.

5.

For
i=1,\dots,n_{e}
and
j=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1
,

\partial_{w}^{2}\varphi(w)=\left\{\begin{split}&\frac{1-\frac{1}{m_{i}}}{(w-w_{i})^{2}}+\dotsm\hskip 227.62204ptw\to w_{i},\\
&\frac{1}{(w-w_{j})^{2}}+\frac{1}{(w-w_{j})^{2}\log\left|\frac{w-w_{j}}{J_{1}^{(j)}}\right|}+\frac{1}{(w-w_{j})^{2}\log^{2}\left|\frac{w-w_{j}}{J_{1}^{(j)}}\right|}+\dotsm\\
&\hskip 301.59938ptw\to w_{j},\\
&\frac{2c_{n}}{w^{3}}+\frac{1}{w^{2}}+\frac{1}{w^{2}\log\left|\frac{w}{J_{-1}^{(n)}}\right|}+\frac{1}{w^{2}\log^{2}\left|\frac{w}{J_{-1}^{(n)}}\right|}+\dotsm,\hskip 71.13188ptw\to\infty\end{split}\right.

Proof.

Parts 2-5 follow from (
300
) and (
304
). Also, the part 1 follows from parts 2-5 and the definition of
Schwarzian derivative
in (
16
).
∎

Remark C.1
.

In this paper, we sometimes need to study the behavior of objects in the case when the point at the infinity is a conical point. So it is good to clear things out in that case as well. In this remark, we assume that the point
w_{n}
is a conical point at infinity. Instead of (
296
) we have:

J(z)=\sum_{k=-1}^{\infty}J^{(n)}_{k}\left(\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}}^{m_{n}}\right)^{k}.

By inverting this expansion, we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}}

\displaystyle=\left(J_{-1}^{(n)}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{n}}}\left(\frac{1}{w}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{n}}}+\frac{\left(J_{-1}^{(n)}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{n}}}J_{0}^{(n)}}{m_{n}}\left(\frac{1}{w}\right)^{1+\frac{1}{m_{n}}}+\cdots,

which, according to the
lemma 3
of
Takhtajan:2001uj
, it implies that in the case of the conical point at the infinity, the accessory parameter becomes

c_{n}\equiv h_{n}J_{0}^{(n)},

(305)

with
h_{n}\equiv 1-1/m_{n}^{2}
.
Next point of interest is the part 3 of lemma
C.1
which implies that for conical points
w_{i}
that are not at infinity, we have

\mathsf{h}_{i}=|J^{(i)}_{1}|^{\frac{2}{m_{i}}}=\frac{4}{m^{2}_{i}}\lim_{w\rightarrow w_{i}}e^{-\varphi(w)}|w-w_{i}|^{-2+\frac{2}{m_{i}}}.

This expression is also subject to a slight change for
\mathsf{h}_{i}
when
w_{i}\to\infty
. Considering
\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}}=(z-z_{i})/(z-\bar{z}_{i})
and
J^{-1}(w)=z
to write
\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}}=(J^{-1}(w)-z_{i})/(z-\bar{z}_{i})
implies that

J^{-1}(w)=z_{i}+2\sqrt{-1}\imaginary z_{i}\left(\left(J_{-1}^{(n)}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{n}}}\left(\frac{1}{w}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{n}}}+\frac{\left(J_{-1}^{(n)}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{n}}}J_{0}^{(n)}}{m_{n}}\left(\frac{1}{w}\right)^{1+\frac{1}{m_{n}}}+\cdots\right).

Now from (
300
) and the equation above we have

e^{\varphi(w)}=\frac{4}{m_{n}^{2}}\left(J_{-1}^{(n)}\right)^{\frac{2}{m_{n}}}\left(\frac{1}{w}\right)^{2\left(1+\frac{1}{m_{n}}\right)}+\cdots.

Accordingly,

\mathsf{h}_{n}=\left|J_{-1}^{(n)}\right|^{\frac{2}{m_{n}}}=\frac{m^{2}_{n}}{4}\lim_{w\rightarrow\infty}e^{\varphi(w)}|w|^{2(1+\frac{1}{m_{n}})}.

(306)

C.1
Quadratic and Beltrami Differentials

In this appendix, we will study the behavior of quadratic differentials, i.e., cusp forms of weight 4, and harmonic Beltrami differentials, automorphic forms of weight (-2,2), near elliptic and parabolic fixed points and connect them with functions on the Riemann orbisurface
O
(see
Kra_1972
;
Lehner_1964
for more details). Let us begin by studying the behavior of quadratic differential
q(z)\in\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(\mathbb{H},\Gamma)
as
z\to\infty
: Parabolic generator
\kappa_{n_{p}}\in\Gamma
with the fixed-point
z_{n}=\infty
has the following normal form (see (
295
)):

\kappa_{n_{p}}(z)=z+\delta_{n},

i.e., it is a translation by
\delta_{n}
. Therefore, the equation
q(\kappa_{n_{p}}z)\kappa^{\prime}_{n_{p}}(z)^{2}=q(z)
satisfied by the quadratic differential
q(z)
can be translated as

q(z+\delta_{n})=q(z).

(307)

It is then easy to verify that the following Fourier series expansion

q(z)=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q_{k}^{(n)}\exp(\frac{2 \pi\sqrt{-1} k z}{|\delta_n|}),

(308)

satisfies the equation (
307
) and therefore represents the behavior of
q(z)
near
z_{n}=\infty
. In order to study the behavior of
q(z)
near the fixed points of other parabolic generators
\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{n_{p}-1}
, we note that the Möbius transformation

\operatorname{PSL}(2,\mathbb{R})\ni\varsigma_{n_{e}+j}(z)=\frac{1}{z-z_{n_{e}+j}},\qquad j=1,\dots,n_{p}-1,

sends that fixed-point to
\infty
and we have
\varsigma_{n_{e}+j}^{-1}\hskip 2.84526pt\kappa_{j}\hskip 2.84526pt\varsigma_{n_{e}+j}(z)=z+\delta_{n_{e}+j}
. As a result, the normal form of the parabolic generators
\kappa_{j}
for
j=1,\dots,n_{p}-1
is given by

\frac{1}{\kappa_{j}(z)-z_{n_{e}+j}}=\frac{1}{z-z_{n_{e}+j}}+\delta_{n_{e}+j},

and we have that
q(z)
satisfies

q\left(z_{n_{e}+j}+\frac{(z-z_{n_{e}+j})}{1+\delta_{n_{e}+j}(z-z_{n_{e}+j})}\right)\frac{1}{\left(1+\delta_{n_{e}+j}(z-z_{n_{e}+j})\right)^{4}}=q(z),

near parabolic points
z_{n_{e}+1},\dots,z_{n-1}
. Therefore, as
z\to z_{i}
the quadratic differential has the following Fourier series expansion

q(z)=\frac{1}{(z-z_{i})^{4}}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q_{k}^{(i)}\exp(-\frac{2 \pi\sqrt{-1} k}{|\delta_{i}|(z-z_{i})}),\qquad i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1

(309)

To study the behavior of differentials near the elliptic fixed points
z_{1},\dots,z_{n_{e}}
, it is easier to first use the unit disk model of the hyperbolic plane. Let
\tau_{i}\in\Gamma
be an elliptic generator of order
m_{i}
with fixed points
z_{i}\in\mathbb{H}
and
\bar{z}_{i}\in\bar{\mathbb{H}}
. Then, the Möbius transformation

\operatorname{PSL}(2,\mathbb{R})\ni\varsigma_{i}(z)=\frac{z-z_{i}}{z-\bar{z}_{i}},\qquad i=1,\dots,n_{e},

(310)

sends the points
(z_{i},\bar{z}_{i})
to
(0,\infty)
and therefor
\varsigma_{i}^{-1}\hskip 1.42262pt\tau_{i}\hskip 1.42262pt\varsigma_{i}(z)
fixes both
0
and
\infty
and should be given by
\varsigma_{i}^{-1}\hskip 1.42262pt\tau_{i}\hskip 1.42262pt\varsigma_{i}(z)=\lambda_{i}z
. For elliptic generators, the
multiplier

\lambda_{i}
can be determined to be the
m_{i}
-th primitive root of unity, i.e.
\lambda_{i}=\exp(2 \pi\sqrt{-1}/m_i)
,
via the use of condition
\tau_{i}^{m_{i}}=\mathbbm{1}
. Therefore, the normal form of the elliptic generators
\tau_{i}
of order
m_{i}
is given by

\frac{\tau_{i}(z)-z_{i}}{\tau_{i}(z)-\bar{z}_{i}}=e^{\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{m_{i}}}\frac{z-z_{i}}{z-\bar{z}_{i}},\qquad i=1,\dots,n_{e}.

The Möbius transformations (
310
) give the standard isomorphism
\varsigma_{i}:\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{D}
; let us denote the coordinate on
\mathbb{D}
by
\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}}=\varsigma_{i}(z)
and the push-forward of the density of Poincaré metric,
\rho(z)=(\imaginary z)^{-2}
, by
\rho(\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}})=4(1-|\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}}|^{2})^{-2}
. Similarly, if we denote the push-forward of
q(z)\in\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(\mathbb{H},\Gamma)
with
q(\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}})\in\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)
, it satisfies

q(\lambda_{i}\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}})\hskip 1.42262pt\lambda_{i}^{2}=q(\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}}).

It is easy to see that the solution to the above equation has the following power series form in
\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}}
:

q(\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}})=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q^{(i)}_{k}\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}}^{km_{i}-2}\qquad\text{as}\quad\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}}\to 0,

(311)

since
\lambda_{i}^{m_{i}}=1
. We can also find the pullback of
q(\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}})\in\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)
to
\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(\mathbb{H},\Gamma)
by using
q(\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}})\differential{\mathsf{u}_{\UD}}^{2}=q(z)\differential{z}^{2}
– i.e.
q(z)=q(\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}})\left(\derivative{\mathsf{u}_{\UD}}{z}\right)^{2}
,

q(z)=-\frac{4(\imaginary z_{i})^{2}}{(z-\bar{z}_{i})^{4}}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q^{(n+j)}_{k}\left(\frac{z-z_{i}}{z-\bar{z}_{i}}\right)^{km_{i}-2}\qquad i=1,\dots,n_{e},

(312)

which satisfies the equation

q\left(\frac{-z\,z_{i}+\bar{z}_{i}\left(\lambda_{i}z+z_{i}+\lambda_{i}z_{i}\right)}{z(\lambda_{i}-1)-\lambda_{i}z_{i}+\bar{z}_{i}}\right)\hskip 2.84526pt\lambda_{i}^{2}\frac{\left(\frac{-z\,z_{i}+\bar{z}_{i}\left(\lambda_{i}z+z_{i}+\lambda_{i}z_{i}\right)}{z(\lambda_{i}-1)-\lambda_{i}z_{i}+\bar{z}_{i}}-\bar{z}_{i}\right)^{4}}{(z-\bar{z}_{i})^{4}}=q(z).

We can summarize the behavior of
q(z)\in\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(\mathbb{H},\Gamma)
near branch points and cusps as

q(z)=\left\{\begin{split}&-\frac{4(\imaginary z_{i})^{2}}{(z-\bar{z}_{i})^{4}}\,\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q^{(i)}_{k}\left(\frac{z-z_{i}}{z-\bar{z}_{i}}\right)^{km_{i}-2}\hskip 19.91684pt(i=1,\dots,n_{e}),\hskip 14.22636ptz\to z_{i},\\
&\frac{1}{(z-z_{i})^{4}}\,\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q_{k}^{(i)}\exp(-\frac{2 \pi\sqrt{-1} k}{|\delta_i|(z-z_{i})})\hskip 14.22636pt(i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1),\hskip 2.84544ptz\to z_{i},\\
&\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q_{k}^{(n)}\exp(\frac{2 \pi\sqrt{-1} k z}{|\delta_n|}),\hskip 113.81102ptz\to\infty.\end{split}\right.

(313)

Using the complex anti-linear isomorphism
\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(\mathbb{H},\Gamma)\cong\mathcal{H}^{-1,1}(\mathbb{H},\Gamma)
given by
q(z)\mapsto\mu(z)=\rho(z)^{-1}\,\overline{q(z)}
,
120
120

120

Equivalently,
q(\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}})\mapsto\mu(\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}})=\rho(\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}})^{-1}\,\overline{q(\mathsf{u}_{\mathbb{D}})}
induces the isomorphism
\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)\cong\mathcal{H}^{-1,1}(\mathbb{D},\Gamma)
.
we have

\mu(z)=\left\{\begin{split}&-\frac{4(\imaginary z)^{2}(\imaginary z_{i})^{2}}{(\bar{z}-z_{i})^{4}}\,\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\bar{q}_{k}^{(i)}\left(\frac{\bar{z}-\bar{z}_{i}}{\bar{z}-z_{i}}\right)^{km_{i}-2}\hskip 14.22636pt(i=1,\dots,n_{e}),\hskip 14.22636ptz\to z_{i},\\
&\frac{(\imaginary z)^{2}}{(\bar{z}-z_{i})^{4}}\,\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\bar{q}_{k}^{(i)}\exp(\frac{2 \pi\sqrt{-1} k}{|\delta_{i}|(\bar{z}-z_{i})})\hskip 22.76228pt(i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1),\hskip 2.84544ptz\to z_{i},\\
&(\imaginary z)^{2}\,\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\bar{q}_{k}^{(n)}\exp(-\frac{2 \pi\sqrt{-1} k \bar{z}}{|\delta_n|}),\hskip 99.58464ptz\to\infty,\\
\end{split}\right.

(314)

where we have used the fact that
\bar{z}_{n_{e}+j}=z_{n_{e}+j}\in\mathbb{R}
for
j=1,\dots,n_{p}-1
. The mapping

q(z)\mapsto Q(w)=(q\circ J^{-1})(w)\,\left(J^{-1}(w)^{\prime}\right)^{2},

(315)

determines a linear isomorphism of spaces
\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(\mathbb{H},\Gamma)
and
\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(O)
and the inverse mapping is given by

Q(w)\mapsto q(z)=(Q\circ J)(z)\,J^{\prime}(z)^{2}.

(316)

The Petersson inner product in
\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(\mathbb{H},\Gamma)
can be carried over to
\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(O)
by setting
\langle Q_{1},Q_{2}\rangle\overset{\text{\tiny\it def.}}{\equiv}\langle Q_{1}\circ JJ^{\prime 2},Q_{2}\circ JJ^{\prime 2}\rangle
for all
Q_{1},Q_{2}\in\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(O)
, so that

\langle Q_{1},Q_{2}\rangle=\iint_{\mathbb{C}}e^{-\varphi(w)}Q_{1}(w)\,\overline{Q_{2}(w)}\differential[2]{w}.

(317)

It follows from Eq.(
313
) and Eq.(
304
) that

\begin{split}Q(w)&=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q_{k}^{(n)}\exp(\frac{2 \pi\sqrt{-1} k}{|\delta_n|} \times\frac{|\delta_n|}{2 \pi\sqrt{-1}} \left(\log(\frac{J_{-1}^{(n)}}{w}) + \frac{c_n}{w} + \cdots\right))\times\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\times\left(\frac{|\delta_{n}|}{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}\left(-\frac{1}{w}-\frac{c_{n}}{w^{2}}+\cdots\right)\right)^{2}\\
&\simeq\left(-\frac{|\delta_{n}|^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{w^{2}}+\frac{2c_{n}}{w^{3}}+\frac{c_{n}^{2}}{w^{4}}+\cdots\right)\right)\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q_{k}^{(n)}\left(\frac{J_{-1}^{(n)}}{w}\right)^{k}\\
&=-\frac{|\delta_{n}|^{2}q_{1}^{(n)}J_{-1}^{(n)}}{4\pi^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{w^{3}}-\frac{|\delta_{n}|^{2}J_{-1}^{(n)}\big(2c_{n}q_{1}^{(n)}+q_{2}^{(n)}J_{-1}^{(n)}\big)}{4\pi^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{w^{4}}+\order{|w|^{-5}}\hskip 5.69046pt\text{as}\quad w\to\infty,\end{split}

and

\begin{split}Q(w)&=\left(-\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{|\delta_{i}|}\frac{1}{\log(\frac{w-w_{i}}{J_1^{(i)}})-c_{i}(w-w_{i})+\cdots}\right)^{-4}\times\\
&\hskip-19.91684pt\times\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q_{k}^{(i)}\exp(-\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1} k}{|\delta_{i}|}\times- \frac{|\delta_{i}|}{2 \pi\sqrt{-1}} \left(\log(\frac{w-w_{i}}{J_1^{(i)}}) - c_{i} (w-w_{i}) + \cdots\right))\times\\
&\hskip-19.91684pt\times\left(-\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{|\delta_{i}|}\frac{-1}{(w-w_{i})\log^{2}(\frac{w-w_{i}}{J_{1}^{(i)}})}+\cdots\right)^{2}\\
&\hskip-19.91684pt=-\frac{|\delta_{i}|^{2}q_{1}^{(i)}}{4\pi^{2}J_{1}^{(i)}}\cdot\frac{1}{w-w_{i}}+\order{|w-w_{i}|^{-2}},\hskip 14.22636pt(i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1)\hskip 2.84544pt\text{as}\hskip 2.84544ptw\to w_{i}.\end{split}

Similarly, near branch points
w_{1},\dots,w_{n_{e}}
we have

Q(w)=(q\circ\tilde{J}^{-1})(w)\left(\tilde{J}^{-1}(w)^{\prime}\right)^{2},

(318)

where
\tilde{J}^{-1}:O\to\mathbb{D}
is the inverse of Klien’s Hauptmodule in the unit disk model of the hyperbolic plane. Using equations (
302
) and (
311
), we get

\begin{split}Q(w)&=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}q^{(i)}_{k}\left(\left(\frac{1}{J^{(i)}_{1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{i}}}(w-w_{i})^{\frac{1}{m_{i}}}-\frac{J^{(i)}_{2}}{m_{i}\left(J^{(i)}_{1}\right)^{2+\frac{1}{m_{i}}}}(w-w_{i})^{1+\frac{1}{m_{i}}}+\cdots\right)^{km_{i}-2}\times\\
&\hskip-14.22636pt\times\left(m_{i}^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{J^{(i)}_{1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{i}}}(w-w_{i})^{-1+\frac{1}{m_{i}}}-(1+\frac{1}{m_{i}})\frac{J^{(i)}_{2}}{m_{i}\left(J^{(i)}_{1}\right)^{2+\frac{1}{m_{i}}}}(w-w_{i})^{\frac{1}{m_{i}}}+\cdots\right)^{2}\\
&=\frac{q^{(i)}_{1}}{m_{i}^{2}J^{(i)}_{1}}\cdot\frac{1}{w-w_{i}}+\order{1}\hskip 28.45274pt(i=1,\dots,n_{e}),\hskip 2.84544pt\text{as}\hskip 5.69046ptw\to w_{n+j}.\end{split}

We can summarize the behavior of
Q(w)\in\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(O)
near conical singularities and punctures as follows

Q(w)=\left\{\begin{split}&\frac{q^{(i)}_{1}}{m_{i}^{2}J^{(i)}_{1}}\cdot\frac{1}{w-w_{i}}+\order{1}\hskip 85.35826pt(i=1,\dots,n_{e}),\hskip 14.22636ptw\to w_{i},\\
&-\frac{|\delta_{i}|^{2}q_{1}^{(i)}}{4\pi^{2}J_{1}^{(i)}}\cdot\frac{1}{w-w_{i}}+\order{|w-w_{i}|^{-2} },\hskip 5.69046pt(i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1),\hskip 11.38092ptw\to w_{i},\\
&-\frac{|\delta_{n}|^{2}q_{1}^{(n)}J_{-1}^{(n)}}{4\pi^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{w^{3}}+\order{|w|^{-4}}\hskip 128.0374ptw\to\infty.\end{split}\right.

(319)

Finally, using the complex anti-linear isomorphism
\mathcal{H}^{2,0}(O)\cong\mathcal{H}^{-1,1}(O)
given by
Q(w)\mapsto M(w)=e^{-\varphi(w)}\,\overline{Q(w)}
, we have

M(w)=\left\{\begin{split}&\frac{\bar{q}^{(i)}_{1}}{4\bar{J}^{(i)}_{1}}\cdot|w-w_{i}|^{1-\frac{2}{m_{i}}}+\order{|w - w_{i}|}\hskip 42.67912pt(i=1,\dots,n_{e}),\hskip 5.69046ptw\to w_{i},\\
&-\frac{|\delta_{i}|^{2}\bar{q}_{1}^{(i)}}{4\pi^{2}\bar{J}_{1}^{(i)}}\cdot|w-w_{i}|\log^{2}|w-w_{i}|+\order{\log^2 |w-w_{i}|}\\
&\hskip 196.32384pt(i=n_{e}+1,\dots,n-1),\hskip 5.69046ptw\to w_{i},\\
&-\frac{|\delta_{n}|^{2}\bar{q}_{1}^{(n)}\bar{J}_{-1}^{(n)}}{4\pi^{2}}\cdot\frac{\log^{2}|w|}{|w|}+\order{|w|^{-2} \log^2|w|}\hskip 28.45274ptw\to\infty.\end{split}\right.

(320)

Using the definition (
86
) as well as equations (
304
) and (
314
), one can independently check the above asymptotic behaviors for
M(w)
.
